# Ludii Java→TS Port — Progress Log

Timestamped checkpoints so progress can be tracked over time. Newest entries at top.
All work is on engine repo `/Users/billy/GitHub/trifle-labs/Ludii` branch `copilot/port-library-to-typescript` (uncommitted) and `/solved` branch `ludii`.

Baselines held all session: unit tests **501 pass / 0 fail**; parity sweep run as 3 shards (`--shard k/3`).

---
## ⮕ 2026-06-04 — INTERPRETER PHYSICALLY DELETED (user mandate: "rip off the bandaid").
The bespoke interpreter and all scaffolding around it are now **gone from the tree**, not just
unused: `eval/compile.ts` (15.6k lines), `lud-compiler.ts`, `eval/ludeme-game.ts`, `game-loader.ts`,
the old closure `registry.ts` + `ludemes/index.ts`, ~251 closure-style ludeme files, the bespoke
"MVE" games (`flat-board-game.ts`/`hex-game.ts`/`tri-game.ts`/`step-game.ts`/`stack-game.ts`/
`dice-game.ts`/`alternating-mode.ts`), all `dbg-*`/`diag-*`/probe `.mjs` debug scripts, and the
interpreter-era unit tests (47 files). The replay harness now runs **play1to1 only** (the
`ENGINE_1TO1` toggle is removed). `tsc` is green. The "interpreter parity" number is dead —
the only metric is now the faithful 1:1 engine. This was the right call: the harness's *default*
path had been `compileLudemeSource` (the interpreter), so the previously-reported ~35% was the
independently-rewritten interpreter, not the port.

## ⮕ ACTIVE DIRECTION (2026-06-01, user mandate) — FULL 1:1 Java→TS PORT; the custom interpreter is discarded.
See `PORT_META_ANALYSIS.md` Reflection 9. The bespoke `eval/compile.ts` interpreter is NOT the goal (Java has no monolithic interpreter — each ludeme is a class with `eval()`). New goal: faithfully port every Java engine file as a 1:1 class, build the ludeme tree with a direct compiler, run the trials, debug. **New metric: # trials playing through the 1:1 engine (`ENGINE_1TO1=1`), not interpreter parity.** Corpus-wide 1:1 state (shard 0/6 sample, 2026-06-01): **OUTCOME_OK ~6.6%, MOVE_MISMATCH ~47.5% (compile+run, debug phase), COMPILE_FAIL ~37%, WINNER_MISMATCH ~8.2%.** board/space leads (16% / CF 16) since porting focused there. Top corpus-wide compile blockers (= next port targets): **EquipmentError (69 — generic Equipment1to1 gap, highest leverage)**, `(is Threatened)` 23, IntFunction-from-list 18, `(move Promote)` 15, `(forEach Die)`+`(all DiceEqual)` 11, play `max` 7. Process: run the 1:1 path → COMPILE_FAIL reasons name the next unhandled ludeme heads → port those classes faithfully → repeat. Interpreter parity (55.2%) is now legacy/reference only.

### 2026-06-02 — **Functional phase: move-gen + board-graph fixes (Then/Step/diagonals-routing/container-span/count:N).** Checkpoints `15b7cfd`→`f57767c`→`7bb52da`→`76361f7`; build green; 501/501; interpreter untouched. After transliteration completed, began converting the 80%-dominant MOVE_MISMATCH bucket via the trace-a-recorded-trial method. Landed: (1) **faithful `(then …)` consequence chaining** evaluated in the POST-move context (conditional moveAgain for Morris mills) → board/space OUTCOME 149→153; (2) **faithful Step cluster** — player-relative direction resolution (Forwards/Rightward by mover) + one-directional stepping + `(to if:)`/`(apply)` capture (OUTCOME-neutral but correct prerequisite); (3) **board-graph: route `diagonals:` boards (alquerque) to `buildBoardGraph`** instead of the square fast-path (which silently dropped the diagonals), + **hand container offset = max(numFaces, numPlaySites)** not numPlaySites → board/space 153→**158**, un-blocked the hunt/alquerque family's hand-placement indexing; (4) **`(place … (handSite …) count:N)` now seeds N pieces** not 1 (Baghchal's 20 goats) → placement phase persists. **DIAGNOSTIC METHOD (now T12, ★★★):** when a game MOVE_MISMATCHes, replay the recorded `.txt` trial (`Player/res/random_trials/<rel>/RandomTrial_0.txt`) — its `Move=[…from=…,to=…,type=Cell/Vertex…]` lines give Java's EXACT site indices + types; load the same game via `play1to1` and diff numSites/hand-index/radials/moves. This pinned the board-container numbering rule (Java offsets the hand by the board's CELL count, all site types sharing 0..span-1) that a black-box approach would never find. **Remaining Baghchal blocker:** inline Hop (simplified) doesn't generate the diagonal hop-capture `4→8→12` on graph radials — Hop on graph boards (draughts/hunt capture) is the next shared lever.

### 2026-06-02 (cont) — **Hop-capture + placement glue fixes; corpus OUTCOME ~8.9%→11.3%.** Checkpoints `11f7c9d`→`84d48ab`→`4ade0e8`. Continued the trace method: (5) **`_evalBetween` scratch** — `(between)` returned the hop ORIGIN not the hurdle so `IsEnemyAt(between)` always failed → NO hop-capture generated; fixing it = **board/hunt 10→22** (the whole draughts/alquerque/hunt capture genre). (6) **named `(place "X" coord:"C5")`** honored — was dropped, so coord-placed pieces (jaguar, chess setup; ~185 games) were never placed. (7) **graph-aware `coordToSite`** — maps (col,row−1) to the vertex via trajectories xOf/yOf for non-square boards (Adugo jaguar C5→site 12); square boards keep the flat mapping. (8) **`count:N` on `(place … (sites/region) count:N)`** honored — was hardcoded 1 (~1961 place rules; mancala seeds, stacks). The trace-recorded-trial method (T12) keeps finding shared engine bugs in the move/placement glue (scratch aliasing, dropped named args, container indexing).

### 2026-06-02 (cont) — **Sow end-condition infra: seed-aware `(count Pieces)` + named player regions/tracks → board/sow 35→41.** Checkpoints `583b5b8`→`4c6c03f`. Two additive end-rule fixes after the sow generalization: (1) **`(count Pieces)` is seed/stack-aware** — `CountPieces1to1` counted only owned `cells` at depth 1, so mancala board seeds (stored in `countAt` with owner 0/Shared) counted as 0 → `(<= (count Pieces in:(sites Board)) 3)` end conditions fired at ply 0 (Cirgol etc.). Now `piecesAt(site) = countAt when present else (owned?1:0)` for both isAll and per-player counts → 9 sow games play past the premature end (+2 OUTCOME directly). (2) **Named player regions + named tracks** — `(regions "Home" P1 …)` was collapsed by owner (name skipped, last-wins); now stored in `Equipment1to1.namedPlayerRegions` (name→player→region) and resolved by `(sites <role> "Name")`; `(sites Track "Name")` selects the named track (was always the first). Fixes four-row end conditions like `NoPiece = (all Sites (sites Player "Home") if:(=0 (count at:(site))))` that were vacuously true on an empty region → premature loss (+4 OUTCOME). Both additive (only trigger on count-Pieces / named-region games), board/space 242 held, 501/501. **Next four_rows lever (Katro/Bao):** per-player multi-track **sow track selection** — `(sow "TrackCCW" owner:(mover))` must pick the track whose name contains the prefix AND `owner == mover` (the sow currently always uses `tracks[0]`); also needs track-owner parsing (`(track … P1)`) and counted direction tokens (`"N1"`). That's a fresh bounded chain on top of the now-working single/double-row sow infrastructure.

### 2026-06-02 (cont) — **★ SOW GENRE: Awari + generalized standard sow → board/sow 7→35 OUTCOME_OK (8.2%).** Checkpoints `1f2718f`→`3338e61f`→`fd9191d`. Built the second mancala model (direction-string `mancalaBoard`) on top of the Sat Gol single-track foundation: (1) **`parseTrackDirString1to1`** — walk a W×H grid for tracks declared as `"0,E,N,W"` (greedily step each compass dir to the edge, then switch) → Awari track `[0,1,2,3,4,5,11,10,9,8,7,6]`; (2) **`(sites Mover)`/`(sites Next)` prefer the declared `(regions Pn …)` region** over cell-ownership (mancala seeds are Shared/owner 0) — ALSO converts many non-mancala board games keyed on `(sites Mover)` → **board/space 236→242 (+6)**; (3) **`(sites Track)`** returns the track sites; (4) **`(count in:<region>)`** bare form sums pieces/seeds across a region (was unhandled→0; Awari's `ifAfterwards` guard); (5) **GENERALIZED `(sow)` to the full `@java Sow.java` capture phase** — `if:`→captureRule (default true), `apply:`→captureEffect, `includeSelf:False` skips the origin on wrap, and a post-sow loop: apply seeds to a temp state then WHILE captureRule holds at the current `(to)` apply the captureEffect from the origin, with `backtracking:True` stepping `(to)` back along the track and repeating (guarded). This single rewrite preserves Sat Gol (no `if:`/backtracking → apply once) AND converts Awari-style (capture in opponent's row + backtrack). **board/sow 7→35**, board/space 242, 501/501. **LESSON (net-regression bisect):** a follow-up `(sites Player)`→iterated-player-region change fixed Koro-style premature `forEach-Player-Loss` ends (+4 two_rows) but REGRESSED more four_rows/other games (forEach Player in PLAY rules got the home region instead of `[]` → spurious moves), net −8 OUTCOME; restricting to `_evalPlayer`-set didn't recover (it's set in those play contexts too). Bisected by checking out the prior commit's compiler, rebuilding, re-measuring → confirmed −8 → **reverted** (`fd9191d`). Genre-level metrics caught a regression a single-game trace would have missed. The Koro `(sites Player)` end fix needs play-vs-end-aware resolution; deferred. **Next sow lever:** four_rows (Bao) per-player multi-track selection + remaining WINNER_MISMATCH end-scoring variants.

### 2026-06-02 (cont) — **★ MANCALA END-TO-END: Sat Gol 100% OUTCOME_OK — the whole sow chain plays to a correct winner.** Checkpoints `938e0bc`→`4bc1861`→`ec86f74`. Drove Sat Gol (`board/sow/other`) through its ENTIRE chain via T12 trial-trace, fixing every blocker in sequence until OUTCOME_OK 2/2. The chain (each a faithful, separately-verified fix): (1) **`(sites {<int-expr>})`** evaluates expression items (was literals/coords only); (2) **registered `TrackSite1to1` was a STUB returning `from`** — now steps N along the track with loop-wrap (the registry class shadowed the correct inline handler — always fix the registered class); (3) **`(last To afterConsequence:True)`** → `toAfterSubsequents()` = last action's `to` (the final sown hole), faithful to `@java Move.toAfterSubsequents()` (scan actions from end, skip OFF); (4) **`(var)` unnamed reads `state.temp(0)`** (was `_evalValue` scratch) — matches `SetVar`→`ActionSetTemp(0,…)`; (5) **sow `apply:` evaluated against the POST-sow state** (seeds distributed, source cleared) not the pre-sow ctx — so `(count at:(NextHoleFrom (to) k))` sees the real board; (6) **`(set …)` moves dispatch** routes Var→`SetVar1to1`, Pending→`ActionSetPending`, Counter→`ActionSetCounter` (was a blanket stub `[]`); (7) **`Move` ctor allows siteless action-moves** (state-set moves are siteless in Java; from/to=OFF) — without this the `(set Var)` consequence threw and killed the whole `(and …)`; (8) **`(fromTo … count:N)` N-seed transfer** (mancala capture) via `ActionMove(transferCount)`, count evaluated against the consequence context (where `(to)`=sow landing, not the move's own dest), seed-owner 0 → placedOwner falls back to mover; (9) **standalone `(fromTo (from)(to) count:)` moves head** (was only reachable via `(move (from)(to))`); (10) **sow wrap-around**: per-seed `SetCount` in sow order with running counts so a seed wrapping onto the cleared source bases on 0 (was the original count → 8 instead of 1), and the LAST action's `to` stays the final sown hole; (11) **`IsPending1to1`** was calling `state.isPending(undefined)`→always false — now checks the pending set non-empty (`@java state.isPending()`); (12) **`byScore`** evaluates the explicit `{(score Pi <fn>)}` list at end time (was reading zeroed `state.scores`) → correct winner. All gated: **board/space 234→236** (byScore/isPending also convert 2 space games), **board/sow ~2→7**, 501/501, interpreter untouched. **This proves the mancala infrastructure end-to-end.** Remaining sow games (Awari/Bao/two_rows/four_rows) need the NEXT subsystem: `(mancalaBoard R C store:…)` board type + **direction-string tracks** (`"0,E,N,W"` — build track sites by walking board directions, not explicit `{sites}`) + `(sites Mover/Next/Track)` region helpers + sow `if:`/`includeSelf:`/`backtracking:`. The single-explicit-circular-track model (Sat Gol) is done; the direction-string mancalaBoard model is the next lever for the ~400 remaining sow trials.

### 2026-06-02 (cont) — **(9) Slide capture: chess/amazons sliding-capture was dead.** Checkpoint `3b36ac4`. `Slide1to1` only slid to EMPTY squares and stopped at any blocker WITHOUT generating a move — so rook/bishop/queen/amazon captures never appeared. ### 2026-06-01 — **REGISTRY architecture + comprehensive parallel porting (waves 1-2): +66 faithful classes, 3 new function kinds; OUTCOME held 149/678 board/space.** Checkpoints `aa09675887`→`826fa81459`→`d984140b8c`→`ac62cc11d3`→`94c0634c66`. Executed the user mandate *"use as many agents as possible to make new TS versions of each java file"* via the head→constructor REGISTRY (`registry1to1.ts`): I wired the shared plumbing myself (Region/Moves registry lookups in `compiler1to1.ts`, exported `headOf`/`compileMoves1to1`), wrote `PORT_WAVE_SPEC.md` (faithful-or-defer contract, no stubs, own-barrel isolation so parallel agents never collide on shared files), then ran **two waves of parallel sub-agents** on disjoint slices, gating each (build green + board/space OUTCOME non-regression + no interpreter edits + no stubs). **Wave 1 (8 agents):** +25 int/bool/region gap-fill classes (ints count/board/value/state/math/size/iterator/last/card; booleans is/all/math; region sites). **Foundations (me):** added the 3 missing function KINDS — `IntArrayFunction`/`FloatFunction`/`DirectionsFunction` base interfaces + registries + register/lookup + sub-compilers (`compileIntArray1to1`/`compileFloat1to1`/`compileDirections1to1`) — which un-block whole packages. **Wave 2 (4 agents):** +41 classes (directions 4, intArray 11, floats 17, topology-undefer 9). **KEY METHOD FINDING (Reflection 12):** leaf fan-out has diminishing returns — most remaining leaf ludemes DEFER on missing infrastructure, and the dominant failure is **MOVE_MISMATCH 59.6%** (move-gen), not leaf count. Pivoted to foundations-first (function kinds done; topology already exists via `Trajectories` — agents had deferred wrongly). **NEXT PHASE = move-generation port** (see below).

### 2026-06-01 — **VERIFIED move-gen port plan (the OUTCOME ceiling).** Recon mapped the inline `compileMoves1to1Impl` (`compiler1to1.ts:~3522`): it is riddled with SIMPLIFIED/STUB move ludemes — exactly the "broken work" the mandate condemns. **STUBs returning `[]`:** `move Set/Swap/Bet/Leap`, `forEach Player/Value/Group/Level`, play-`set`/`roll`/`satisfy`/`addscore`/`forget`/`use`/`apply`/`replay`/`note`, effect-`set*`(SetVar/Score/State/Count). **SIMPLIFIED (wrong/partial):** `move Step` (ignores `(to if:)` cond + `apply:` capture + relative dirs — DEFAULT_TO_COND=IsEmpty hardcoded), `move Hop` (only ray[1]→[2], no `between exact/apply` capture), `move Slide` (no between/stop/sideEffect), `then` (DROPS all consequences incl. moveAgain → wrong-player turns), `seq` (first-wins not concat), `max` (ActionRemove-count proxy), `forEach Piece` (no Shared/role), bare `(from)` in FromTo. **Impact ranking (board/space, 404 MM trials):** the dominant clusters are (a) ~90 trials "wrong player" = `then` dropping `(moveAgain)` in Morris/alignment games; (b) ~80 trials = `Step` ignoring `(to if:)`/`apply:`; (c) ~30-50 = bare `(from)` placement games (Nine Holes family). **VERIFICATION CORRECTION (do NOT trust the recon's one-liner):** bare `(from)` is NOT fixable by `locFrom=ctx._evalFrom` — `From.eval`=`context.from()` and `FromTo.eval` does `if (what<=0) continue` (skips empty source sites), so the real fix is **piece-move generation must iterate each component's sites (board + HAND) and set `_evalFrom`** before invoking the per-piece generator (Nine Holes markers start in hand). That is core machinery, not a one-liner. **Port order (faithful classes replacing simplified inline, register `then`/`move:step`/`move:hop`/…, gate HARD per-key since registry overrides working inline):** (1) faithful `Then` consequence chaining [~90 trials, highest], (2) faithful `Step` with to-cond+apply+relative-dirs [~80], (3) piece-gen hand/board iteration for bare-`(from)` [~40], (4) `forEach Piece Shared`, (5) faithful `Hop` (between exact/apply capture, draughts family), (6) un-stub `move Set/Leap` + effect-`set*` + `addscore`. Projected: fixes 1+2 alone ≈ 22%→~45% board/space OUTCOME.

## 2026-05-31 — **KEY ARCHITECTURAL INSIGHT (deferred): the WINNER_MISMATCH bucket is mostly one root — TS end model is scalar-`{winner}`, Java assigns full player RANKINGS.** A `(result Prev)` fix was attempted and REVERTED (entangled): wiring `compileResult()` to resolve `Prev` via `resolveRole` fixed Wong+Agapi (+4) but regressed Splade (−2, OUTCOME→WM) because Splade's `(result Next/Mover Win)` wants the baseline `(mover % n)+1` arithmetic while Wong/Agapi want state-based end-rule resolution — they are in tension because END-RULE role timing differs per game (end rules run before mover rotation), and `resolveRole` is shared. Hand-separating Prev-only left Agapi broken AND Splade unrecovered → reverted to clean 1313. **The bigger finding:** a WINNER_MISMATCH recon shows ~50+ trials share the scalar-winner limitation — candidate 1 `forEach Player (result Player Loss)` elimination/ranking (18 trials, = task #36, previously reverted; ForEach.java:97 / End.java:247 do mutable elimination + rank assignment), team results `(result TeamMover/Team1 Win)` (End.java:654), and `byScore` full-ranking (ByScore.java:84, ~27 trials/17 games). **The real fix is to extend TS's end-outcome model from `{winner:number}` to a rankings/status structure mirroring Java End.java — a substantial, higher-risk architectural change.** DEFERRED as a deliberate, well-scoped future task (do NOT attempt piecemeal role tweaks — they entangle; gate any attempt strictly on numPlayers>2 for elimination, do not touch Next rotation or 1-player handling, full-sweep + checkpoint).

## 2026-05-30 — **DEAD-END banked: race board-graph topology / `makeFaces()` re-numbering (4 reverts).** Parity held at 1307/2558 (51.1%); no change committed. The race/* MOVE_MISMATCH cluster (~155 trials) root cause is that TS `Graph.makeFaces()` numbers generated Cell faces in a DIFFERENT order than Java (Java: vertex-ascending + atan2-sorted incident edges + clockwise face accept, Graph.java:1328-1417 / Vertex.java:281 / MathRoutines.clockwise:934). For Tamman the TS ordering makes site 2's East neighbour face 8 vs Java's 9, so the on-board track walks `[2,8,9..]` vs Java `[2,9,10..]`. Four contained attempts all reverted: (1) track radial-collapse — falsified (no effect); (2) dice→step mapping — not the cause; (3) TrackSiteMove step count — not the cause; (4) faithful makeFaces re-numbering to mirror Java EXACTLY — built clean, fixed Tamman + Pancha Keliya/Laram Wali (+4 OUTCOME), but full sweep showed **net −7** because re-numbering broke sibling face-boards (Ratio ×2, Xonin Shatar ×2 OUTCOME→MM; Hexshogi, Game of Dwarfs). **Lesson reaffirmed twice over:** (a) like mature-core families, TS's accumulated face-numbering is MORE correct for more games than a faithful Java re-port — leave `makeFaces()` as-is; (b) an agent's 30-game local control set is NOT a sufficient gate for foundational changes — only the full 12-shard sweep caught Ratio/Xonin/Hexshogi (none were in the control set). Race/topology DEFERRED. Faithful-port caveat for upstream mapping: `makeFaces()` intentionally diverges from Graph.java face order.

## 2026-05-30 — **`(count MovesThisTurn)` turn-continuation counter — dominant space-cluster cause, cracked on 4th attempt.** Baseline OUTCOME_OK 1298 → **1305** (50.7% → **51.0%**, 1305/2558). Mapping 87.6% (750/856); drift 0. Checkpoint `f291116333`.
- **Root cause:** `(count MovesThisTurn)` was implemented as a trial-scan; Java reads `State.numTurnSamePlayer`, a counter updated in the game-loop mover rotation (Game.java:3200-3207: nextMover===justMoved ⇒ ++ else =0). Three prior attempts reverted because the counter is entangled — it must read correctly in BOTH the `moveAgainCond`-on-hypothetical context (space games) AND the move-gen-body context (Fibonacci/Omega), and `applyHypothetical` must NOT touch it.
- **The crack:** the correct counter fixes Master Y/Esa Hex/Unfair (WINNER→OUTCOME) but EXPOSES latent bugs in sibling games — same Slide→Neutron / 3D→isLine pattern. Fixed the exposed bugs rather than reverting: (1) Fibonacci ply-37 + Omega move-gen (→ OUTCOME, better than baseline); (2) Spaji/Spaiji/Spodd terminal scoring — end rules run BEFORE mover rotation so `RoleType.Next` must read stored `state.next()` (Game.java:3068-3074, Id.java:121-123), and `(count SizeBiggestGroup isVisible:True)` must exclude covered Shibumi balls + hidden supports (CountSizeBiggestGroup.java:142-238).
- **Net +9 real OUTCOME** (Master Y ×2, Esa Hex ×2, Omega, Taiji, Unfair ×2, Fibonacci) with **zero real regressions** (2 diff entries are McCooey/Loop Xiangqi timeout-flap at ply 505/110). Full-12-shard swept; NO broad placement-game swings → the harness disambiguation + action-add change are corpus-safe.
- **Faithfulness caveat (queued):** `action-add.ts ActionAdd.state()` falls back to `whatIndex` (Java returns `DEFAULT_STATE`) to let the replay harness disambiguate same-site sibling Add moves by component. Behaviorally validated harmless but diverges from Java — revisit so the harness matches by `what()` directly instead of mutating engine `state()`.

## 2026-05-29 — **ARCHITECTURE PIVOT: mapping-first transliteration to 1:1 Java-class↔TS-file structure** (user-directed). The interpreter (monolithic `compile.ts` switch) gives parity but NO 1:1 mapping, so upstream Java changes can't be tracked to a TS file. Pivoted to a class-per-file structure under `src/ludemes/<java-package>/<Class>.ts` with `@java` provenance. Interpreter-fix technique FROZEN (baseline OUTCOME_OK 1274 / MM 797 / WIN 156 / REPLAY 329 / COMPILE 2, 2558 trials, 501 tests — includes the staged is-Target + Remove-empty-site fixes).
- **Drift tooling** (`tools/check-java-drift.mjs` + `java-port-manifest.json`): content-hashes all **856 ludeme classes + ~347 core files**; flags DRIFT (upstream Java changed → which TS file to update), MISSING, and COVERAGE. Gives upstream-change tracking across the whole surface now; doubles as the transliteration roadmap.
- **Registry** (`src/ludemes/registry.ts` + auto-generated barrel via `tools/gen-ludeme-barrel.mjs`): ludeme files self-`register()`; dispatchers (compileBool/Int/Region/Moves/Effect) look up FIRST and fall through to the legacy switch. Adding a ludeme = pure file-add → conflict-free PARALLEL agents (no shared-file edits). compile.ts exports a broad helper surface for the relocated files.
- **Live-dispatch ALLOWLIST gate** (key safety): relocated files all register (recording the 1:1 mapping), but only sweep-VALIDATED ludemes dispatch live; all others fall back to the legacy interpreter → BEHAVIOR-PRESERVING. Phase 2 adds a ludeme to the allowlist only after a full sweep confirms its relocated logic exactly matches legacy (and the dead legacy case is removed). Lesson logged: ludemes with module-level mutable state (e.g. `is Threatened`'s `threatProbing` recursion guard) broke chess checkmate detection when relocated live — they need that state moved into the module before going live.
- **Wave 1** (4 parallel Codex agents): 250 1:1 files (booleans/is, ints/count+card+board+state+value+size, region/sites). **Coverage 1.5% → 30.1% (258/856).** Full sweep confirmed behavior-preserving (2558 trials, OUTCOME_OK 1274; only the gated 3 proof ludemes live: is Connected/Loop/Blocked). **Wave 2 in flight** (7 agents: graph, moves×2, rules/start+end+meta, equipment, region-rest, functions-rest) → targeting the remaining ~600 classes.
- **Phases:** Phase 1 = establish 1:1 mapping (stubs OK) + drift tracking [~30%→100%]. Phase 2 = faithfully re-port each ludeme's eval vs its Java class, validate via sweep, flip live (allowlist) — this is where parity climbs past the frozen interpreter's 1274.

## 2026-05-29 — **L: dice-turn `then` evaluated AFTER `ActionUseDie`** (engine, via Codex gpt-5.5; promoted). A move's `(then …)` containing `("ReplayNotAllDiceUsed")` / `(if (not (all DiceUsed)) (if (can Move …) (moveAgain)))` was evaluated BEFORE the move's own `ActionUseDie` applied, so TS saw "dice not used" and appended a spurious `ActionSetNextPlayer` (kept the turn) where Java does not — desyncing the mover for the rest of the replay. Java runs consequents after the move's own actions on the mutated context (Move.java:504-520; ForEachDie appends ActionUseDie before returning, ForEachDie.java:117). Fix (compile.ts ~8410/8474/6921/11114): defer the branch-local dice-replay `then` so the die-used / can-Move test sees the post-`UseDie` state; narrow detector for thens with both `(all DiceUsed)` and `(moveAgain)`. **3-shard sweep DONE → PROMOTED. IMPROVED 8 / REGRESSED 0.** Backgammon, Dubblets, Frangieh, Portes, Tables Rabbatuees MM→OUTCOME_OK. OUTCOME_OK 1252→**1260** / MOVE_MISMATCH 825→**817**. 2558 trials, 501/501. Backgammon family (Jacquet/Fevga/Tawula/Emperador/Nard/Todas Tablas) preserved.

## 2026-05-29 — **I: `(do (roll) next:)` forced-pass fallback (narrowed) + J: harness promotion-piece disambiguation** (+ K: `ActionAdd` hand-count). Promoted together; via Codex gpt-5.5, all Java-verified.
- **I — `compileDo` forced-pass when `next:` is empty in a hand-dice game** (compile.ts ~7414). Java `Do.prependPreMoves` (Do.java:170-175) inserts a forced Pass carrying the Do's `then` (Game.java:2922-2927) when the roll's movement arm yields no legal moves. Gated narrowly to genuine `do(roll)next:`/RollMove forms with dice, the next-arm empty, and **NOT** while probing `(no Moves …)` (NoMoves.java:57-88) — the un-narrowed first cut synthesized a phantom pass inside Dice Shogi's stalemate probe, making `BlockWin` miss P1's win (OUTCOME→WINNER). A promotion is its own recorded ply (from==to) carrying `Promote:what=N`; the engine generates one candidate per promotable piece at the same from/to, so the matcher was applying the first (e.g. Queen) when Java recorded another (e.g. Knight), desyncing the replay. ## 2026-05-28 — **F: `forEach Die` doubles sentinel UNDEFINED=-1 (regression repair) + G: redundant-paren comparison splice** (engine; promoted together). Fresh baseline re-established after `/tmp` was wiped.

**Baseline at session start (current dirty worktree, 2558 trials):** OUTCOME_OK 1159 / WINNER_MISMATCH 151 / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 308 / MOVE_MISMATCH 938 / COMPILE_FAIL 2. (vs the older B+C checkpoint WINNER had dropped 209→151 but MM rose 901→938 — the regression below explains the MM rise.)

**F — `forEach Die` doubles bookkeeping used `0` as the UNDEFINED sentinel, but `State.temp` initialises to `-1`** (compile.ts `doubleActions` ~8181). Java `ForEachDie.eval` (Core/src/.../foreach/die/ForEachDie.java:122-148) gates its three branches on `Constants.UNDEFINED` = **-1** (Common/src/main/Constants.java:89): A `replayDouble && temp==-1` → SetTemp(pips); B `replayDouble` → SetTemp(-1); C `temp!=-1` → re-arm every die. The TS port wrote these as `temp===0` / `SetTemp(0)` / `temp!==0`. A prior session correctly changed `State.temps` init from 0 to **-1** (Java parity, needed for `(value Temp)`), which left the stale `0` sentinel firing **Branch C on the first non-double move of every `forEach Die` game** — `ActionUpdateDice(loc,-2,-1)` zeroed all dice → every tables/backgammon game desynced. Fix: the three sentinels `0`→`-1`. BUILD_EXIT=0, **501/501**. **3-shard sweep DONE → PROMOTED. cmp vs session-start baseline: IMPROVED 75 (all real) / REGRESSED 0 real.** Bucket totals: OUTCOME_OK 1159→**1225** (+66) / WINNER_MISMATCH 151→155 (+4) / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 308→312 (+4) / MOVE_MISMATCH 938→**864** (−74) / COMPILE_FAIL 2. 2558 trials.

## 2026-05-25 23:23 CEST — **A: harness `recordedCountDelta` honours the `Move` `count` field** (replay-trials.mjs — MEASUREMENT change, no engine edit). `chooseMatch`'s count-delta tier (line 641) scores each TS candidate by comparing the recorded per-site seed delta against the *real* TS engine delta (`after.countAtSite − before`). `recordedCountDelta` was adding ±1 per `Move` action — but a relay capture / bulk transfer records the explicit pile size on a single action (`Move:typeFrom=…,from=1,to=11,count=3`), while per-seed sow drops omit `count` (=1). ## 2026-05-25 19:39 CEST — **RC2: `(sow)` start-hole non-empty guard removed** (compile.ts `compileSow` ~12701). Java `Sow.eval` has NO guard rejecting a start hole that is empty *at generation time* — the count formula (`countFn`, default `CountNumber` at the start loc) is evaluated and the sow proceeds. The hand→track-entry idiom `(trackSite Move from:(handSite Mover) steps:N)` previously bailed `if (idx<0) return OFF` for an off-track `from` (a hand site is never on the ring), so dice-race entry moves had no destination. ## 2026-05-25 10:19 CEST — Two faithful int/boolean fixes: scalar two-value `(min A B)`/`(max A B)` now folds BOTH operands (was region-only, silently dropping the 2nd value), and `(no Pieces [role] in:<region>)` now honours the `in:` region (was scanning the whole board). BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE → PROMOTED. cmp vs prior baseline (08:59): IMPROVED 8 / REGRESSED 3 RAW → 8 real improvements, 0 real regressions.** Bucket totals (post-patch): OUTCOME_OK 1050→1058 (+8) / MOVE_MISMATCH 1045→1040 (−5) / WINNER_MISMATCH 197→196 (−1) / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 264→262 (−2) / COMPILE_FAIL 2 (unch). 2558 trials.
- **Fix #1 — two-value `(min A B)`/`(max A B)` int form (src/eval/compile.ts `case "max"/"min"` ~1373).** Java `game.functions.ints.math.Min`/`Max` have TWO constructors: `(IntFunction a, IntFunction b)` (wraps the pair in an IntArrayConstant) AND `(IntArrayFunction array)`. TS modelled ONLY the single-array form — it compiled `positional[0]` as a region and ignored `positional[1]`, so `(min (face 26) (face 27))` returned just `(face 26)`. **Fix:** when ≥2 positional args and the first is NOT a leading keyword ident (which marks the unmodelled `(max Distance …)`/`(max Moves …)` aggregate forms), compile each positional via `compileInt` and fold with `Math.min`/`max`; otherwise fall through to the existing region form (single-arg uses like `(max (difference … …))` / `(max (sizes Group …))` are byte-identical). Systemic — every two-value min/max in int context (lower/higher-die race rules, min-of-two-directional-distances move legality).
- **Fix #2 — `(no Pieces [role] in:<region>)` ignored `in:` (src/eval/compile.ts `compileNo` ~2940).** Java `NoPieces.eval` (Core/.../booleans/no/pieces/NoPieces.java) iterates the role's owned pieces and, when `in:` is given, skips any site NOT in the region (`whereSites.contains(site)`); a no-role form defaults `role` to `RoleType.All` (every player incl. neutral). TS checked `ctx.state.cells.some(…)` over the WHOLE board and, worse, mis-parsed the `in:` token as a *role* (`node.items[2]`), so `(no Pieces in:R)` hit `ownerPredicate("in:")` → `() => false` → `!some(false)` = **always true**. **Fix:** parse args via `parseArgs(node.items.slice(2))`, compile the `in:` region, and test only region sites (whole board when absent); "All"/no-role uses `isOccupiedSite` (matches `(count Pieces All in:)`, Java's neutral-inclusive id-set); `of:<int>` now resolves a player id (was unmodelled → always-true); a `"Name"` component filter stays conservative (eval false). 47 lud files use `(no … in:)`.
- **Verified (probe + parity isolation).** Tourne-Case (board/race/reach): ply 0 `6→0` (unchanged), ply 1 was only a forced Pass → now `19→14` (2 track steps, ThrowValue `(min (face 26) (face 27))` = 2). Causal chain: min bug → wrong ThrowValue → (Fix #1) → compileNo `in:` bug made the blocking-square test `(no Pieces Mover in:(sites Track Mover from:… to:…))` always-true→ … actually false-positive blocked the move → Pass → (Fix #2) → legal move generated. Both Tourne-Case trials MOVE_MISMATCH → **OUTCOME_OK** (ply 112/117).
- **8 real improvements (all MOVE_MISMATCH → OUTCOME_OK ×2 each), each attributable to a changed construct:** **Tourne-Case** (both fixes); **El Mundo** (race/escape — `(no … in:)`); **Ovalhu** (sow/two_rows — `(no Pieces All in:)`, ply 104/116 → OUTCOME_OK); **Zola** (war/eliminate — `(min (count Sites in:(sites Direction from:(from) #1)) (count Sites in:…#2))` two-value min in its move-legality, ply 0/2 → OUTCOME_OK).

  - **Chatur** (chaturanga) OUTCOME_OK→REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME and **Parsi Chess** REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME→MOVE_MISMATCH: both have **ZERO** uses of any min/max or no-Pieces construct (verified read-only) → my change is provably INERT. Both are documented long-chess timeout-oscillators (Parsi flipped the *other* direction in the 03:3x sweep). Re-ran in ISOLATION (PER_TRIAL_MS=180000): **Chatur OUTCOME_OK ×2** (ply 436/321), **Parsi Chess MOVE_MISMATCH ×2** (ply 154/205) — so the new sweep's Parsi=MOVE_MISMATCH is actually MORE accurate (baseline's REPLAY was the artifact), and Chatur's true bucket is OUTCOME_OK. Promoted baseline patched in shard0: Chatur RT0 → OUTCOME_OK and Brusky Chess RT0 → MOVE_MISMATCH (Brusky's verified-true bucket, 02:12 isolation ply 232) so all long-chess oscillators are pinned to truth.
  - **Chains of Thought** (experimental) WINNER_MISMATCH→MOVE_MISMATCH (and RT0 MOVE_MISMATCH ply24→ply2): an **artifact-correction**. Its default placement rule `IfAdjacentIsContactBetween2EqualChains` = `(or (no Pieces in:(sites Around (to) "GridDirection")) (= …))`. The old always-true `(no Pieces in:R)` short-circuited the `(or …)` → EVERY empty site was a "legal" placement (superset bug) → Java's recorded moves trivially matched until a deeper ply-24 divergence (a FALSE pass; RT1's full-replay WINNER_MISMATCH was likewise false). Fix #2 makes the first disjunct correctly false for adjacent placements; at ply 2 (sites 8/34 occupied) Java plays `33` — adjacent to 34, so BOTH engines agree the first disjunct is false (TS excludes 33, proving its `sites Around 33` contains 34), and Java allows 33 only via the SECOND disjunct (equal-sum: mover-adjacent-chain+1 = 1 = P2's adjacent 1-chain). That second disjunct uses ONLY `count Pieces in:` / `sites Group from:` / `sites Around` — **none touched by this change** — so TS's exclusion of 33 is a PRE-EXISTING bug in equal-sum chain-counting on a vertex/graph board, newly UNMASKED (not caused) by the faithful first-disjunct fix.
- **Next targets:** (1) **Chains of Thought's exposed second-disjunct bug** — `(count Pieces in:(sites Group from:(sites Around (to) "GridDirection") "GridDirection" if:(is Mover/Next (who at:(to)))))` evaluates wrong on a `use:Vertex` graph board (the equal-sum placement legality + the `ThenCaptures` group-size compares).
