## 2026-05-25 08:59 CEST — `collectDefines` now harvests `(define …)` nested ANYWHERE in the post-option tree (was top-level + curly only) → defines injected through `(option … args:{… <rules>} …)` arg slots resolve, fixing the silently-dropped teleport/relocation consequence in 58 Holes & the Hounds-and-Jackals race family. BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE → PROMOTED. cmp vs prior baseline (07:22): IMPROVED 3 / REGRESSED 1 RAW → 2 real improvements (58 Holes ×2), 0 real regressions (Chatur + Brusky Chess flips are verified timeout-oscillator artifacts, inert to this change).** Bucket totals (post-patch): OUTCOME_OK 1048→1050 (+2) / MOVE_MISMATCH 1047→1045 (−2) / WINNER_MISMATCH 197 / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 264 / COMPILE_FAIL 2 (all unch). 2558 trials.
- **Root cause (src/lud-defines.ts `collectDefines`).** It collected `(define "Name" body)` macros only from the file top level and from *curly* `{…}` blocks (`else if (item.delimiter === "curly")`), never descending into round-paren lists. Ludii's `(option "Variant" … args:{ <hand> <region> <pieceMove> <map> <rules> } { (item … <(define "Teleportation" …) (rules …)> …) })` declares the teleport macro *inside the `<rules>` arg slot*. Pipeline order is `applyOptions` → `expandDefines`, so once the selected item's `<rules>` substitutes into `<Variant:rules>` (a **direct child of `(game …)`**, 58 Holes.lud:218), the `(define "Teleportation" …)` lands as a child of the round-paren `(game …)` node — which `collectDefines` skipped. The body was never registered, so the call site `("Teleportation")` (a zero-arg `("Name")` invocation that *expansion* handles fine) had no macro to expand and survived as a bare `(string "Teleportation")` list. `compileEffectAction` saw `head=undefined`, matched no case, and dropped it — so the move's `(then (and ("ReplayInMovingOn" …) ("Teleportation")))` consequence lost its teleport entirely.
- **Why it surfaced as a deep MOVE_MISMATCH (not at the teleport ply).** The harness only checks each move's primary from/to, so the missing teleport ActionMove was invisible at the ply it occurred (58 Holes ply 32: Java `Move(6→9), Move(9→7)` via map pair 9→7; TS emitted only `Move(6→9)`, leaving the piece at 9). The board silently desynced — two plies later (ply 34) Java moves `from=7` (the teleported-to site, empty in TS) which TS can't generate → "No matching TS move".
- **Fix.** `collectDefines` now recurses into EVERY list child (`else { collectDefines(item, into); }`), matching Java `Expander`'s extract-every-`(define`-token-from-the-source semantics. The `(define …)` branch still only collects (intentionally does not recurse into a macro's own body). `expand`/`expandList` already strip nested defines and re-walk substituted bodies, so the sole gap was collection breadth. Safe because `applyOptions` runs first and removes unselected option items — no cross-ruleset macro leaks into the tree.
- **Verified (TELEPORT_DEBUG + isolation):** 58 Holes RandomTrial_0 & _1 — teleport move-effect now compiles (`head=move`, byte-15263) and fires at runtime (`src=9 dst=7`, `src=38 dst=36` = map pairs `(pair 9 7)`/`(pair 38 36)`) → MOVE_MISMATCH(ply 34/…) → **OUTCOME_OK** (plyReplayed 196/189). Systemic: every game that injects a define through an option arg slot (race/escape Hounds-and-Jackals family + connection/experimental games surveyed read-only) now gains its previously-dropped macros — most simply already worked because their option defines happened to be top-level; this repairs the arg-slot-injected ones.
- **The cmp's 1 "regression" + 1 incidental "improvement" are both timeout-oscillator artifacts, NOT this change.** Chatur (chaturanga, OUTCOME_OK→REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME, basePly 321/newPly 303) and Brusky Chess (chess, MOVE_MISMATCH→REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME) BOTH have **0 `(option …)`** and **no define-in-arg-slot** (verified read-only) → `collectDefines`' broadened recursion collects the identical macro set for them ⇒ provably inert. Both are ~140–436-ply games that oscillate buckets at the per-trial timeout under sweep load (documented 05:31 & 02:12). Re-ran Chatur in isolation (PER_TRIAL_MS=180000): **OUTCOME_OK ×2** — true bucket. Promoted shard1 patched to restore Chatur=OUTCOME_OK and Brusky Chess=MOVE_MISMATCH (their verified-true buckets) so the baseline stays accurate. → ZERO real regressions.
- **Next targets:** remaining race/escape MOVE_MISMATCH that are NOT teleport-define — Ashta-kashte ply177 & Ashtapada ply34 (per-game dice-race move-gen edge cases, traced earlier to specific dice/trackSite states, not a shared opening fix); deferred count-mode ActionAdd (unblocks `sowEffect:` for Ngulungu/Quendo/Mwendo/Buqruru/Ga); Match/Subgame mode (Bul/Tavli/Julbahar/Mahbouseh/Mughrabieh/Grand Trictrac). UNCOMMITTED.

## 2026-05-25 07:22 CEST — Sown seeds now inherit the ORIGIN hole's owner (was hard-coded `numPlayers+1`) → `(piece "Seed" Each)` mancala ownership correct. BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE → PROMOTED. cmp vs prior baseline (06:30): IMPROVED 3 / REGRESSED 0 — clean, zero-regression faithfulness fix.** Bucket totals: OUTCOME_OK 1046→1048 (+2) / MOVE_MISMATCH 1050→1047 (−3) / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 263→264 (+1) / WINNER_MISMATCH 197 / COMPILE_FAIL 2 (unch). 2558 trials.
- **Root cause (src/eval/compile.ts `compileSow`, ~line 12498).** Every dropped seed used `seedOwner = env.sowSeedOwner ?? numPlayers+1`. Java `Sow.eval` (Sow.java 277-280) sows via `ActionMove.construct(start → to)` — it MOVES the seed component out of the origin hole, so each destination inherits the *moved piece's owner*, i.e. the origin hole's current owner. For `(piece "Seed" Shared)` games that owner IS the neutral `numPlayers+1`, so the old constant was right by coincidence. But for `(piece "Seed" Each)` games — Ngulungu, Chiana wa Kunja, Quendo, Mwendo (4-row mancala; `(place "Seed1" (sites Bottom)) (place "Seed2" (sites Top))`) — the mover sows their OWN Seed1/Seed2, so sown holes must stay owned by the MOVER. Hard-coding `numPlayers+1` (=3 in a 2-player game) corrupted `(who at:(to))` on every sown hole; these games' selector `"LeftMost"` = `(trackSite FirstSite from:(trackSite FirstSite if:(not (is Mover (who at:(to))))) if:(is Mover (who at:(to))))` reads `(who at:)` to find the mover's leftmost seed-hole → it skipped the just-sown holes and picked the wrong origin → MOVE_MISMATCH at ply 2.
- **Fix.** Read the live source owner once at sow time — `const srcOwner = ctx.state.cells[from] ?? 0; const seedOwner = srcOwner > 0 ? srcOwner : fallbackOwner;` — and use it for both the origin's `-startCount` removal and every `+1` drop. Unifies both seed kinds faithfully: Shared games keep `numPlayers+1` (the source's neutral owner — behaviour unchanged), Each games carry the mover's seed owner. Bucket-neutral on Shared mancala (verified: no Shared-game regressions in the sweep), strictly more faithful, fixes the Each family.
- **Verified (DEBUG_PLY + sweep):** Ngulungu RandomTrial_0 & _1 hole 1 now `o1` (was `o3`); recorded Java move `from=1` now matches TS → MOVE_MISMATCH@ply2 → **OUTCOME_OK@ply18** (both promoted in sweep). Chiana wa Kunja MOVE_MISMATCH ply2 → ply97 (same bucket; a SEPARATE deeper divergence remains). Quendo MOVE_MISMATCH ply2 → WINNER_MISMATCH (replays fully now). The 3rd sweep IMPROVE (Parsi Chess MOVE_MISMATCH→REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME, ply 201) is incidental run-to-run variance — a chaturanga game with NO sow construct — not attributable to this fix, but harmless (an improvement, not a regression).
- **`sowEffect:` port ATTEMPTED then REVERTED (shelved with a code NOTE).** `compileSow` ignores the `sowEffect:` named arg (5 games: Ngulungu, Quendo, Mwendo, Buqruru, Ga). Java `Sow.eval` evals it at EACH hole BEFORE the drop (Sow.java 266-272) — Ngulungu's `sowEffect:(if (and (!= (mover) (what at:(to))) (is Occupied (to))) (and (remove (to) count:(count at:(to))) (add (piece (id "Seed" Mover)) (to (to)) count:(count at:(to)))))` flips an opponent-owned hole to the mover's seed mid-sow. **Direct port (compile `sowEffect:` + append its actions before each drop, eval against the original board) REGRESSED Ngulungu OUTCOME_OK→WINNER_MISMATCH and Quendo WINNER→MOVE** and was reverted (BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500, Ngulungu back to OUTCOME_OK confirmed). Root cause: the conversion's `(remove … count:N)` + `(add … count:N)` use the generic *stack-based* add/remove actions, which don't conserve seed COUNT when composed with the mancala count-model (`ActionAddCount`); per-player totals drift and the `(no Pieces Player)` end test flips the winner — moves still all match, only the final tally diverges. **Faithful support needs count-mode (seed-count-preserving) add/remove first.** Until then the owner-fix keeps `(who at:)` correct (what `"LeftMost"` reads), so most corpus trials replay fine; residual divergences are Chiana-wa-Kunja ply97 (a separate `(do (forEach Site …) next:(sow))` capture-to-hand mechanism, NOT sowEffect) and Quendo/Mwendo winners. UNCOMMITTED.

## 2026-05-25 06:30 CEST — Graph perimeter now traced PER connected component (was a single global ring) → `(sites Centre)`/`(sites Perimeter)`/`(sites Outer)`/`(sites Corners)` correct on merged/disjoint boards. BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE → PROMOTED to rolling baseline. cmp vs prior baseline (05:31): IMPROVED 1 / REGRESSED 0 — a clean, zero-regression faithfulness fix.** Bucket totals: OUTCOME_OK 1045→1046 (+1) / MOVE_MISMATCH 1051→1050 (−1) / WINNER_MISMATCH 197 / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 263 / COMPILE_FAIL 2 (all unch). 2558 trials. Shard 0 was byte-identical (0 changed trials), confirming single-component boards are unaffected.
- **Root cause (src/eval/graph/graph.ts `makeFaces`).** It enumerated every face cycle via a half-edge walk, then took the SINGLE most-negative-area (clockwise/unbounded) cycle as the entire board perimeter. On a board whose play sites form several disconnected blocks — e.g. two-row mancala from `(merge {(rectangle 2 3) (shift 5 0 (rectangle 2 3)) (shift 3 0.5 (rectangle 1 2))})` — that global ring covers only the largest block, so `perimeterSites()` and the geometric `centreSites()` derived from it were wrong. Java `MeasureGraph.measurePerimeter` traces ONE perimeter per connected component (`createNextPerimeter` loops over uncovered leftmost vertices), drops any perimeter whose start lies inside another (island within a hole), and unions the rest.
- **Fix.** `makeFaces` now (1) union-finds connected components over the edge list, (2) selects each component's most-negative cycle as its outer boundary, (3) drops a ring lying inside another's polygon (new `pointInPolygon` helper, Java `MathRoutines.pointInPolygon`), (4) stores per-component rings in a new `perimeterRings` field and exposes `perimeterVerts` as their flattened de-duplicated union. `cornerVertices` measures corners on EACH ring independently (a concatenation has meaningless turning points); `trim`/`reorder`/`withTransformedCoordinates`/clear keep `perimeterRings` in lockstep. **Single-component boards are byte-identical** (one component ⇒ the per-component most-negative cycle IS the old global one; one ring ⇒ identical corner/centre output) — confirmed by shard-0's 0 changed trials — so the change is inert for the ~99% that already worked and only repairs multi-block boards (which previously had a truncated perimeter).
- **Concrete win.** Fergen Gobale (board/sow/two_rows): `(regions "Central" (sites Centre))` resolved to ~the left block's centre instead of the two central single-row holes (12,13). The sow's `apply:` suppresses `(moveAgain)` only when the last seed lands on a Central hole; with Centre misplaced, TS fired moveAgain on the ply-0 sow-from-8 (last seed → hole 12, central) and kept P1 as mover where Java rotates to P2 → MOVE_MISMATCH at ply 1. Now MOVE_MISMATCH(ply 1) → **OUTCOME_OK (ply 34)**. (Sibling `(sites Centre)` two-row games — Omangunta Peeta, Bechi, Luuth, Kanji Guti — were already OUTCOME_OK; their corpus trials don't exercise the central-landing path. The 2nd Fergen Gobale trial now progresses to a deeper MOVE_MISMATCH at ply 31 — the next sow-relay/capture divergence to chase.)
- **Known minor divergence (noted, not yet ported):** Java's `followPerimeterClockwise` emits a degenerate perimeter for a component with <3 vertices (e.g. a lone 2-hole central block); the half-edge walk yields no cycle for those, so such vertices are absent from `(sites Perimeter)`. The geometric centre still lands correctly (those holes are interior to the union of the larger blocks), so no observed behavioural effect — flagged for a future faithfulness pass.

---

## 2026-05-25 05:31 CEST — Two faithful fixes: `(remove … count:N)` now removes N pieces (was always 1), and `ownerPredicate("TeamMover"/"TeamNonMover")` now resolves the mover's TEAM (was `() => false`, making `(no Pieces TeamMover)` always TRUE → instant ply-1 wins in EscapeTeamWin/CaptureAllTeam games). BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE (04:52/05:06/05:20) → PROMOTED to rolling baseline `/tmp/cur-ofall-*` (05:31). cmp vs prior baseline (03:3x): IMPROVED 1 / REGRESSED 18 RAW — but 17 are verified `plyReplayed=1` instant-win ARTIFACT-CORRECTIONS and the 18th (Chatur) is a confirmed timing artifact (patched out of the promoted baseline), so ZERO real regressions.** Bucket totals: OUTCOME_OK 1049→1045 (−4) / WINNER_MISMATCH 210→197 (−13) / MOVE_MISMATCH 1034→1051 (+17) / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 263 (unch) / COMPILE_FAIL 2 (unch). 2558 trials. Genuine improvement: **Nyout** WINNER_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK (bogus ply-1 win → real full replay to ply 100).
- **Fix #1 — `(remove <site> count:N)` ignored `count:` (compile.ts, two handlers).** Java `Remove.eval` (Core/.../nonDecision/effect/Remove.java:105-160) reads `countFn` (default `IntConstant(1)`) and emits `count` single `ActionRemove`s. TS's effect-position `remove` handler (~10849) and the moves-generator `case "remove":` (~6663) both hard-coded count=1, so `(remove (last To) count:(count at:(last To)))` (Ti's custom mancala opening — empty the whole hole) removed only ONE seed. **Fix:** parse `count:` via `compileInt` in both handlers; emit `new ActionRemove({ to: s, count })` (count≤0 → no-op in effect path / treated as 1 in the moves path). `ActionRemove.apply` already supports `countValue` (action-remove.ts:48-74: `pile > count ? withCountAt(pile−count) : clear`; default 1). Idiom appears in ~18 lud files (war/sow/space/race). Bucket-neutral on the sow cluster (the count-delta harness tiebreak still can't disambiguate Ti's identical-from/to opening), but strictly more faithful and a prerequisite for any game that empties a multi-seed hole.
- **Fix #2 — `ownerPredicate("TeamMover"/"TeamNonMover")` fell through to `() => false` (compile.ts ~5188).** The role regex `/^Team(\d+)$/` requires a digit, so the literal roles `TeamMover`/`TeamNonMover` never matched → predicate always false → `(no Pieces TeamMover)` always TRUE → `("EscapeTeamWin")` = `(if (no Pieces TeamMover) (result TeamMover Win))` fired at ply 1, ending every such game instantly with the wrong winner. **Fix:** added a `TeamMover`/`TeamNonMover` case mirroring Java team semantics — `moverTeam = ctx.state.valuePlayer(ctx.mover)`; a cell `c` is same-team iff `moverTeam>0 ? valuePlayer(c)===moverTeam : c===ctx.mover` (teamless games fall back to identity = the mover alone). Affects 22 games (EscapeTeamWin ×9, CaptureAllTeam ×9, +4 literal uses). `compileResult`'s `(result TeamMover Win)` was already correct (unknown role → mover).
- **The 18 raw cmp "regressions" — 17 are ARTIFACT-CORRECTIONS, 1 is a timing artifact.** The TeamMover fix REMOVES bogus ply-1 instant-wins. Verified on THIS sweep's data: 17 of 18 regressed trials had `plyReplayed==1` in the BASELINE (a real Chaupar/Pachisi/Nyout/Sokkattan/Petol/Asi-Keliya/Nebakuthana/Pahada-Keliya/Kawade-Kelia game runs dozens-to-100 plies — ply 1 = bogus). With the fix these games no longer win-at-ply-1; they now replay forward and diverge deeper (mostly to MOVE_MISMATCH on the underlying dice-race move-gen, a SEPARATE open root). Removing a false OUTCOME_OK/WINNER_MISMATCH that was wrong is faithful progress even though the raw bucket rank drops. The 18th (**Chatur** OUTCOME_OK→REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME, basePly=436 newPly=435) is NOT a TeamMover/`count:` game (verified: end = `("Checkmate" "King_noCross")` + `(count Pieces Next)`, play = `(do (forEach Piece) ifAfterwards:…)`, ZERO Team/remove-count constructs) — it is the SAME known oscillating timeout artifact documented in the 01:26 entry (436-ply chess game, ±1 ply at the 20 s per-trial cap; sibling unaffected chaturanga 12x12/14x14 games showed identical run-to-run plyReplayed variance this sweep). Re-confirmed in isolation (PER_TRIAL_MS=120000): **Chatur OUTCOME_OK ×2 @ plyReplayed=436/321**, identical to baseline. Promoted shard0 patched to restore Chatur=OUTCOME_OK so the baseline stays accurate. → ZERO real regressions.
- **Next targets (mapped read-only this sweep; ply-0 MOVE_MISMATCH cluster = 194 trials):** (1) **Dice-race opening move-gen (Tâb/Trictrac/backgammon family — Julbahar/Mahbouseh/Mughrabieh/Grand Trictrac/Tavli/Bul subgames).** Identical bogus `from=0..3` ply-0 signature; traced to the subgame's `(do (if (NewTurn) (roll)) next:(if (can Move ("Move")) …))` + `(forEach Die replayDouble:True)` + `(trackSite Move from:#1 steps:(pips))` opening — NOT the match wrapper (the harness loads the real subgame; recorded cells go up to 25). Partial port exists (Grand Trictrac do/next at compile.ts:6540). PROBE `DEBUG_PLY=0` on Julbahar to see dice/pips/trackSite state. Best bounded systemic lead. (2) **Graph/tiling board games** (Sim, Dots and Boxes, Hamiltonian Maze, ConHex, Defector Y) — TS builds a differently-indexed graph; large board-construction subsystem, out of scope. (3) **Per-game move rules** (Chameleons start-placement/Hop, etc.) — heterogeneous, per-game probes. UNCOMMITTED.

## 2026-05-25 03:3x CEST — `(count Steps … <region>)` now takes its 2nd arg as a REGION and returns the MIN distance (was treating it as a single site). BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE (03:46/04:00/04:1x) → PROMOTED to rolling baseline `/tmp/cur-ofall-*`. cmp vs prior baseline (03:0x): IMPROVED 2 / REGRESSED 0. Bucket totals: OUTCOME_OK 1047→1049 (+2) / MOVE_MISMATCH 1036→1034 (−2) / WINNER_MISMATCH 210 / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 263 / COMPILE_FAIL 2 (unch). 2558 trials.** Improved: **Center** MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK ×2.
- **Bug (`compile.ts`, `(count Steps …)` ~816):** Java `CountSteps.eval` (Core/.../count/steps/CountSteps.java:75,93-122) takes `site1` (an IntFunction) plus a **RegionFunction** `region2`, and returns the MINIMUM step distance from site1 to any site in the region (default = precomputed `distancesToOtherSite` matrix; site1<0 or empty region → 0). TS compiled BOTH the last two args via `compileInt`, so any region 2nd-arg form was broken. Center's first-move legality `(count Steps All (to) (sites Perimeter)) <= (count Sites … Occupied)` — at ply 0 RHS=0, so legal = sites whose nearest-perimeter distance ≤ 0 = ALL perimeter sites — collapsed to a single legal site → MOVE_MISMATCH @ ply 0.
- **Fix:** compile the 2nd arg via `compileRegion` (the session's earlier IntFunction→1-element-region auto-promotion means the common two-site form `(count Steps (from) (to))` stays EXACTLY `stepDistance(site1, to)`), eval = min `stepDistance` over the region; s1<0 / empty region → 0; non-empty but all-unreachable → `OFF` (preserves the prior two-site unreachable return). Behavior-preserving for all single-site 2nd-arg uses (count Steps is in ~70 lud files); only multi-site region forms change. Sweep confirms 0 regressions.
- **Next targets (unchanged from below + still open):** Arimaa hand-site/setup-order (ply0 Java `from=69`, TS `from=64` — DISTINCT root, not count Steps); Match/Subgame wrapper mode; Backgammon ply-140 deeper desync; big clusters (war 270 / sow 207 / space 189 MM). UNCOMMITTED.

## 2026-05-25 02:12 CEST — `forEach Die` no longer mis-applies a hoisted `forEach Site` `then` to `noMoveYet` (forced bear-off) moves. BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE (02:30/02:44/02:58) → PROMOTED to rolling baseline `/tmp/cur-ofall-*` (03:0x). cmp vs prior baseline (02:10): IMPROVED 3 / REGRESSED 0 real (2 chess flips are confirmed timing artifacts, see below). Bucket totals: OUTCOME_OK 1044→1047 (+3) / MOVE_MISMATCH 1037→1036 (−1) / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 265→263 (−2) / WINNER_MISMATCH 210 (unch) / COMPILE_FAIL 2 (unch). 2558 trials.** Distinct games improved (all → OUTCOME_OK, which a timeout cannot fake — requires full correct replay + winner match): **Backgammon**, **Fevga**, **MughrabiehSubgame**.
- **Bug (`compile.ts`, `forEach Die` + `forEach Site` interaction):** the backgammon/tables family encodes bear-off as `(forEach Die (forEach Site (sites Occupied by:Mover) (if … "RemoveAPiece"/"MoveAPiece") noMoveYet:(firstMoveOnTrack … "RemoveAPiece") (then "ReplayNotAllDiceUsed")))`. `forEach Die`'s `collectNestedThens` (~7689) recurses INTO the `forEach Site` clause, hoists its `(then "ReplayNotAllDiceUsed")` into `allThens` (~7733), strips it from the compiled inner, and `emit()` re-applies it (post-die-spend, so `(all DiceUsed)` resolves) to EVERY move from `inner.generate()` — INCLUDING the `noMoveYet` fallback moves the `forEach Site` generator returns at line ~7536. Java `ForEachSite.eval` (Core/.../foreach/site/ForEachSite.java:86-91) `return`s `elseMoves.eval(context)` on the no-move-yet path BEFORE the `then()` application at line 89, so a forced bear-off (overshoot → `firstMoveOnTrack` escapes the furthest home piece) gets NO turn-retention. TS wrongly stamped `moveAgain=true` → the mover kept the turn instead of rotating → deep-ply MOVE_MISMATCH ("No matching TS move" — TS has the wrong mover, e.g. Backgammon ply 130, Ashta-kashte 177, El Mundo 407).
- **Fix (faithful):** module `WeakSet<Move> foreachSiteNoMoveYetMoves`; the `forEach Site` generator tags the fallback moves at line ~7536 before returning them; `forEach Die`'s `emit()` computes `applicableThens = foreachSiteNoMoveYetMoves.has(m) ? siblingThens : allThens` — so a `noMoveYet` move gets only DIRECT siblings of `forEach Die` (matching Java's `ForEachDie.then()`, null here), never the hoisted `forEach Site` then; main-body moves keep every then. Games with no `forEach Die`+`forEach Site noMoveYet` nesting are untouched (set stays empty → `allThens` for all). 500/500 unit tests; no `forEach Die` family regressions (Nard/Daldos/Ad elta stelpur/Doblet all unchanged in the sweep).
- **Result:** Backgammon MOVE_MISMATCH×2 (ply 130) → OUTCOME_OK×1 + MOVE_MISMATCH×1 (now ply 140 — pushed 10 plies deeper; the remaining ply-140 `mover=2` desync is a separate deeper root). Fevga + MughrabiehSubgame fully resolve.
- **The 2 cmp "regressions" (Brusky Chess, Korkserschach: REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME→MOVE_MISMATCH) are TIMING ARTIFACTS, not real.** Both are chess variants with ZERO dice/`forEach Die`/stack/`level:`/`items:` (verified read-only) → BOTH this change and the prior whatStacks change are provably inert for them. Re-ran in ISOLATION (PER_TRIAL_MS=120000, no load): Brusky Chess **MOVE_MISMATCH ×2** (ply 157/232), Korkserschach MOVE_MISMATCH (ply 464) + REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME. So their TRUE bucket is MOVE_MISMATCH; the prior whatStacks-sweep REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME (recorded as an "improvement" in the 01:26 entry) was ITSELF the artifact (those slow chess games timed out before reaching the mismatch ply under that sweep's load). This sweep is MORE accurate; promoting corrects the baseline. **CORRECTION to the 01:26 entry: Brusky Chess ×2 / De Vasa Chess / Korkserschach ×2 were NOT real whatStacks improvements — they are deep MOVE_MISMATCH chess games that oscillate buckets by timeout. The real whatStacks wins were Tower of Hanoi, Owasokotz, Es-Sig, Sig wa Duqqan, Bizingo.**
- **Next targets (surveyed read-only after promote — race family now OUTCOME_OK 93 / MOVE_MISMATCH 211, of which 143 mismatch at ply<20):**
  1. **Special-geometry race-board site-ordering (NEW lead, possibly shared root, HIGH leverage).** Several non-dice ply-0 race games offer the right move SHAPE at the WRONG site, with TS site indices SHIFTED BELOW Java's: **Arimaa** ply0 Java `from=69→5`, TS offers 16 moves all `from=64` (tops out at 64); **Center** ply0 Java `from=45,to=45` (a placement, from==to), TS offers `from=0,to=0`. Java references HIGHER site indices than TS generates → TS board has fewer/differently-ordered sites for these special boards (star/hex/special geometry). Opposite direction to the 22:40 Kawade-Kelia fix (there TS had MORE cells); likely a vertex/cell ENUMERATION-ORDER mismatch in `graph.ts` for these board shapes. PROBE: dump Java vs TS site coords/ordering for Arimaa + Center boards; check `(place …)` start indices. If shared, fixes a cluster.
  2. **Match/Subgame mode** (Grand Trictrac, Julbahar, Mughrabieh, Tavli, Mahbouseh — all ply-0, stubbed to a 3×3 placeholder). Their standalone subgames already replay (MughrabiehSubgame now OUTCOME_OK via the noMoveYet fix), so only the match WRAPPER is missing. Large multi-file feature (notes `/tmp/match-mode-target.md`).
  3. Backgammon-family deeper desync (now ply 140+, `mover` still diverges after the bear-off fix — another turn-rotation/dice-consumption edge).
  4. Big heterogeneous clusters: war 270 MM, sow 207 MM, space 189 MM.
  UNCOMMITTED.

## 2026-05-25 01:26 CEST — Per-level `what` stacks (`whatStacks`) + `(place Stack items:{…})` placement + level-aware `(what/who at:s level:L)` + stack-aware `ActionMove`. BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE (01:40/01:54/02:08) → PROMOTED to rolling baseline `/tmp/cur-ofall-*` (02:10). cmp vs prior baseline (01:07): IMPROVED 13 / REGRESSED 0 real (the lone flip is a confirmed timing artifact, see below). Bucket totals: OUTCOME_OK 1040→1044 (+4) / WINNER_MISMATCH 207→210 (+3) / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 259→265 (+6) / MOVE_MISMATCH 1050→1037 (−13) / COMPILE_FAIL 2 (unch). 2558 trials.** Distinct games improved: **Tower of Hanoi** (canonical e2e) MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK ×2; **Owasokotz** MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK ×2; **Es-Sig** + **Sig wa Duqqan** (Houmt Taourit / Houmt al-Arbah) MOVE_MISMATCH→WINNER_MISMATCH ×3; **Bizingo**, **Brusky Chess** ×2, **De Vasa Chess**, **Korkserschach** ×2 MOVE_MISMATCH→REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME (deeper, higher bucket).
- **The gap (4 parts, all faithful to Java `ContainerStateStacks`):**
  1. **State had no per-level `what`.** `stacks[site][level]` stored only the per-level OWNER; `whatAtSite` returned a single top `what` and `(what at:s level:L)` ignored `level:`. A multi-DISTINCT-piece stack (Tower of Hanoi's Counter9/Counter4 disks; the snakes-and-ladders pawn piles) could not be represented — a buried disk could not keep a different `what` from the one above it.
  2. **`(place Stack items:{…} <loc>)` was UNPARSED.** With `Stack` present the start parser read `labelNode = item.items[2]`, which lands on the `items:` keyword node (not a string), so no branch fired and the whole placement was silently dropped → 0 pieces → `forEach Piece`/`(sites Occupied by:Mover)` found nothing → only a Pass at ply 0. (This was the biggest shared ply-0 "only Pass" root: ~10 games.)
  3. **`(who at:s level:L)`/`(what at:s level:L)` ignored `level:`** (returned top owner/what), so `MoveToEmptyOrOccupiedByLargerPiece`'s `(- (what … level:(topLevel …)) (who … level:(topLevel …)))` size compare had no real per-level data to read.
  4. **`ActionMove` was not stack-aware** — it cleared the whole source cell, wiping the pieces buried under the moved one.
- **Fix:**
  - `state.ts`: added optional sparse `whatStacks?: readonly (readonly number[])[]` parallel to `stacks` (default = length-n empty arrays; threaded through `with()` unchanged unless explicitly patched). `withStackPush(site, owner, what?)` now materialises + maintains the `what` column (lazily — only when `what≠owner` or a column already exists, back-filling lower levels from their owners) and refreshes top `whats[]`; `withStackPop` pops the column and refreshes top `whats[]`. New `whatAtSiteLevel(s, L)` (per-level `what`, falls back to per-level owner, then flat top-`what`) and `whoAtSiteLevel(s, L)` (per-level owner, **falls back to the cell's top owner when L is at/beyond the stored stack** — essential so count-pile games like Gobblet that store only the visible top keep the pre-change top semantics).
  - `compile.ts`: `(what at:s level:L)`→`whatAtSiteLevel`, `(who at:s level:L)`→`whoAtSiteLevel`; no-`level:` paths unchanged.
  - `ludeme-game.ts`: parse `(place Stack items:{labels} <loc>)` (loc = `coord:"A1"` / bare int / region-or-IntFn list); resolve each label to its (owner, what) bottom→top; apply loop builds a real per-level `stacks[]`+`whatStacks[]` at the site (top owner→`cells`, top what→`whats`, height rides in stack length so `countAt` stays 0). Materialises `stacks`/`whatStacks` into the start State only when at least one such stack was placed (every other site mirrors `fillStacks`' flat default → nothing else changes).
  - `action-move.ts`: when either endpoint carries a per-level `what` column (genuine distinct-piece stack — only `items:` games ever populate `whatStacks`), relocate the TOP piece via `withStackPop(from)` + `withStackPush(to, topOwner, topWhat)` instead of the flat clear. Gating on the **destination** too is required so the one piece whose component id equals its owner (Hanoi's smallest disk, unmaterialised at its own site) still pushes a level onto a real stack rather than being absorbed into the flat same-owner `countAt` merge.
- **Verified:** Tower of Hanoi solves end-to-end (3-disk optimal sequence; final `whatStacks[2]=[3,2,1]`, `over=true`) → OUTCOME_OK ×2. Regression canary **Gobblet Gobblers** (count-pile gobbling, no `items:`): briefly regressed WINNER_MISMATCH→MOVE_MISMATCH from the `who`-level change, FIXED by the `whoAtSiteLevel` top-owner fallback → back to WINNER_MISMATCH ×2 (= baseline). Spot-checks vs baseline: Lasca MM×2 (same), Sig wa Duqqan 2×MM→2×WM + 2×WM (improved), Es-Sig (improved), Gyan Chaupar advanced ply-0→ply-4/5 (deeper, bucket-neutral). 500/500 unit tests throughout.
- **The lone "regression" (Chatur OUTCOME_OK→REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME) is a confirmed TIMING ARTIFACT, not real.** Chatur.lud has NO `stack`/`level:`/`(place Stack)`/`items:` (verified read-only) → whatStacks is provably inert for it. Re-ran the whole chaturanga family in ISOLATION (PER_TRIAL_MS=60000, no sweep load): **Chatur OUTCOME_OK ×2**, identical to baseline. The flip was a slow-game timeout under 3-shard parallel load (166 s for 14 chaturanga trials). Promoted shards were patched to restore Chatur=OUTCOME_OK so the baseline stays accurate. → ZERO real regressions.
- **Remaining (deferred, separate roots):** the race-cluster games (Gyan Chaupar, Sig wa Duqqan, Ishighan, Tugi-Epfe…) now PLACE their stacks and advance several plies but still mismatch deeper — their `(move (from (from) level:(level)) (to (mapEntry …)))` track moves + stick-dice `(count Pips)`/throw-value-map are a distinct root (not placement). UNCOMMITTED.
- **Next target (VERIFIED root, read-only, during this sweep — Task #12): `forEach Die` mis-hoists the `forEach Site` `then` onto `noMoveYet` bear-off moves (board/race/escape family, 219 MM / 368).** Backgammon-family deep-ply MOVE_MISMATCH ("No matching TS move" at ply 130/177/407): in `compile.ts`, `forEach Die`'s `collectNestedThens` (~7675) recurses INTO `(forEach Site … noMoveYet:… (then "ReplayNotAllDiceUsed"))`, hoists that `then` into `allThens` (7701), and `emit()` applies it to ALL moves from `inner.generate()` — INCLUDING the `noMoveYet` bear-off moves returned at 7528. Java `ForEachSite.eval` (Core/.../foreach/site/ForEachSite.java lines 86-91) returns `elseMoves.eval()` BEFORE the `then()` application at line 89, so `noMoveYet` moves get NO turn-retention. TS wrongly stamps `moveAgain=true` → keeps the wrong player moving → deep-ply mismatch. FIX (faithful): tag `noMoveYet` moves at 7528 (module WeakSet); in `emit()` apply the hoisted nested thens ONLY to non-tagged moves (siblingThens + useDice still apply to all). Implement as isolated change, build, 500/500 unit, probe Backgammon ply-130, race/escape filter sweep, promote on 0 regressions.

## 2026-05-25 (CEST) — `compileRegion` now promotes ANY IntFunction in a region slot to a one-element region (Java IntFunction→RegionFunction auto-promotion). BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE (00:42/00:54/01:06) → PROMOTED to rolling baseline `/tmp/cur-ofall-*` (01:07). cmp vs prior baseline (00:21): IMPROVED 4 / REGRESSED 0. Bucket totals: OUTCOME_OK 1038→1040 (+2) / WINNER_MISMATCH 205→207 (+2) / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 259 (unch) / MOVE_MISMATCH 1054→1050 (−4) / COMPILE_FAIL 2 (unch). Improved games: Tic-Tac-Die MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK (both trials, fully solves); Minesweeper MOVE_MISMATCH→WINNER_MISMATCH (both trials, higher bucket). Make Muster / Fractal advanced ply-0 but stayed MOVE_MISMATCH at a deeper ply (bucket-neutral, more faithful).**
- **Bug (`compile.ts compileRegion` default case, ~line 3999):** the region compiler named the common int heads explicitly (`+ - * / max min mod % var value coord mapEntry trackSite site to from between …`) but its `default` THREW `Unsupported region ludeme: (count …)` for any other int head. The dice-placement family writes the destination as a bare IntFunction — e.g. Tic-Tac-Die `(do (roll) next:(if (is Empty (count Pips)) (move Add ("PlayerPiece") (to (count Pips))) …))`, where `(count Pips)` names the single destination cell. `compileRegion((count Pips))` threw → the whole `(move Add …)` threw → `compileDo`'s fail-soft `next:` catch fell back to **roll-only**, so the only generated move at ply 0 was the bare `(roll)` (from=-1,to=-1) → harness saw a "only a Pass"-shaped move → MOVE_MISMATCH @ ply 0.
- **Fix:** in the `default` case, `try { compileInt(node) }` and on success return a singleton region (`s >= 0 ? [s] : []`); on failure re-throw the original unsupported-region error. Safe because `compileInt` itself THROWS on a genuinely unknown head (no permissive fallback — verified at compile.ts:1474), so an un-portable region still surfaces as a compile error rather than being silently mis-promoted. General, faithful fix (mirrors Java promotion) — fixes the whole class (`count`, `if`, …), not just `count`.
- **Confirmed (probe, current dist):** Tic-Tac-Die ply-0 now generates a real `(move Add)` at the die-named cell (was bare roll); Make Muster and Fractal likewise now generate a real Add (`0->0`). Others in the ply-0 "only Pass" cluster (Ringo, Shut Off His Lights, Chex, Nodal Chess, Trax, Tower of Hanoi, Millieu) have DIFFERENT roots and still only-Pass — see next-target note.
- **Next target (scoped read-only during this sweep): `(place Stack items:{…} <loc>)` placement + per-level `what` stacking.** Biggest remaining SHARED ply-0 "only Pass" root (~10 games: Gyan Chaupar, Es-Sig, Sig wa Duqqan ×2, Ishighan, Owasokotz, Tugi-Epfe, Tower of Hanoi, Li'b el-Merafib, Sik). The `items:{…}` placement form is currently UNPARSED in `ludeme-game.ts` (labelNode lands on the `items:` keyword node, isString fails, placement silently dropped → no pieces → forEach Piece finds nothing → Pass). Java `PlaceCustomStack.eval` places each item bottom→top at a coord/loc/int site, building a stack of DISTINCT components. TS gap: State has `stacks[]` (per-level OWNER) but NO per-level `what` — `whatAtSite` returns one top-`what`, and `(what at:s level:N)` ignores `level:`. Tower of Hanoi (clean 1-player puzzle = canonical e2e test) needs per-level `what` because the top piece's SIZE changes as pieces pop. `(forEach Piece)` iterates by site-owner (`cells[s]`), NOT levels, so the FROM side only needs the site owned + the correct top-`what` maintained across push/pop. **Plan:** add backward-compatible `whatStacks?:number[][]` to State (parallel to `stacks`); thread through constructor/`with`/`syncStacks`; make `whatAtSite(site, level?)` + push/pop + ActionMove/ActionAdd stacking level-aware; parse `items:{…}` in `parseStartRules` (loc forms: `coord:` / bare int / IntFn like `("ExternalSite")`/`(handSite P1)` / optional `Edge "EastDoor"`), populate `whatStacks` bottom→top. Test Tower of Hanoi e2e first (should fully solve), then sweep for 0 regressions vs the owner-only stacking games (Lasca/Focus/backgammon). Notes also in `/tmp/dice-race-onlypass-target.md`.

## 2026-05-24 23:46 CEST — `"SameTurn"` (`(is Prev Mover)`) no longer spuriously TRUE on the FIRST throw of a `(do (roll) next:(move … (then (if ("SameTurn") …))))` turn. BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE (23:56/00:08/00:20) → PROMOTED to rolling baseline `/tmp/cur-ofall-*` (00:21) on FAITHFULNESS grounds. cmp vs prior baseline (23:01): IMPROVED 1 / REGRESSED 0; the only bucket flip is Shogi MOVE_MISMATCH→REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME — the SAME timing-artifact game as the 23:01 entry (Shogi reads `"SameTurn"` only at its top-level play rule, never in a do-roll prologue, so this change CANNOT affect it; the flip is CPU-load-dependent bucketing, this time in the "improved" direction). Bucket totals: OUTCOME_OK 1038 (unch) / WINNER_MISMATCH 205 (unch) / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 258→259 / MOVE_MISMATCH 1055→1054 / COMPILE_FAIL 2 (unch). The REAL gain is intra-bucket: 10 trials (the 5 ply-1 Maya games × both trials) advance ply 1 → ply 25–51 (verified base-vs-new ply diff; 0 went shallower).**
- **Bug (`compile.ts compileMoveLudeme` then-fold, ~line 10091):** the 23:01 fix computed `prevMover` from `trial.lastMove()?.mover` — correct for top-level generation, but WRONG inside a `(do (roll) next:X)` prologue. `("RollMove" m)` = `(do (roll) next:m)`; `compileDo`'s next-path builds `tempCtx = ctx.applyHypothetical(rollMove)` and generates the `next:` arm there. After the hypothetical roll, `tempCtx.trial.lastMove()` is the **intra-move `(roll)`** (mover = the current player), so the then-fold recomputed `prevMover` = mover → `(is Prev Mover)` spuriously TRUE on the turn's *first* throw. The Maya stick-dice family (`A K'aak'il`/`Aj Sakakil`/`Aj Sayil`/`Aj Sina'anil`/`Aj T'iwil`) sets `(if ("SameTurn") (set Pending))` inside that Pass-then → TS set Pending one ply early, flipping ThrowPhase→MovePhase before the mandatory second throw, so TS generated a real piece move at ply 1 where Java rolls+passes again. MOVE_MISMATCH @ ply 1.
- **Fix:** prefer the already-stashed inherited value — `const prevMover = ctx.frame.prevMover ?? (ctx.context.trial.lastMove()?.mover ?? 0);`. `applyHypothetical` (eval-context.ts:690) already captures the **pre-prologue** `state.prev()` into `frame.prevMover` (Java: a roll is an action *within* the compound move and never updates `state.prev()`). Top-level generation leaves `frame.prevMover` undefined → falls back to `trial.lastMove()` exactly as before (Pereauni/Choro path preserved — both re-verified OUTCOME_OK).
- **Result (targeted replay):** the 5 ply-1 Maya games now advance **ply 1 → ply 25–51** (A K'aak'il ply 25/30, Aj Sakakil ply 51) — cleared the ply-1 barrier; remaining mismatches are deeper game-specific throw-ordering/accumulation divergences (e.g. A K'aak'il ply 25: Java moves a board piece 2→6, TS still moves from hand 19→{1,3}). Verified at the action level: A K'aak'il ply-0 Pass = `[Roll,Pass,RememberValue,SetNextPlayer]` (no SetPending, matches Java); ply-1 Pass = `[…,SetPending]` (matches Java). 500/500 unit tests; Pereauni + Lango (Choro) still OUTCOME_OK. UNCOMMITTED.
- **Next-target candidates (surveyed read-only during the sweep):** (1) **dice-race "only Pass" at ply 0** — new cluster (race/reach: Celticator, Es-Sig, Gyan Chaupar, Pente Grammai, Sig wa Duqqan, Tourne-Case) where TS generates only a Pass vs Java's real dice-move; shared stick-dice `(count Pips)` / throw-value-map / `NextSiteOnTrack` hypothesis — notes at `/tmp/dice-race-onlypass-target.md` (PROBE a stick-dice game first; needs engine free). (2) **sow/mancala family** (~207 MOVE_MISMATCH, mostly deep) — biggest cluster; re-survey first-divergence post-SameTurn fixes since some were `(is Prev)` bugs (Pereauni was). (3) **Match/Subgame mode** (6 games, `/tmp/match-mode-target.md`). (4) one-offs: **Tugi-Epfe** use:Edge merged-board start placement (piece not placed; numSites=40 edges, cells[0] empty), **Hackenbush** missing cascade-remove of edges disconnected from ground. The deeper Maya divergence (ply 25–51) is now game-specific throw-ordering/accumulation, not the ply-1 bug.

## 2026-05-24 23:01 CEST — Sow `"SameTurn"` (`(is Prev Mover)`) no longer spuriously TRUE in a move's `(then …)` fold. BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE (23:01/23:13/23:25) → PROMOTED to rolling baseline `/tmp/cur-ofall-*`. vs prior baseline (22:40): MOVE_MISMATCH 1058→1055 (−3), OUTCOME_OK 1034→1038 (+4), REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 259→258 (−1); WINNER_MISMATCH 205, COMPILE_FAIL 2 unchanged. cmp IMPROVED 4 / REGRESSED 1 — but the lone "regression" is a confirmed timing artifact, NOT caused by this change (see below), so ZERO real regressions.** New baseline totals: OUTCOME_OK 1038 / WINNER_MISMATCH 205 / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 258 / MOVE_MISMATCH 1055 / COMPILE_FAIL 2 (2558 trials).
- **Bug (`compile.ts compileMoveLudeme` then-fold, ~line 10091):** when folding a `(move … (then …))` at generation time, TS records the candidate move `m` as the throwaway trial's last move (so `(last From)`/`(last To)` resolve). But that polluted `(is Prev Mover)` — the `"SameTurn"` idiom — to see **m's own mover** as the "previous" mover, making `"SameTurn"` spuriously TRUE on a fresh single-move turn. Pereauni's sow `count:(if ("SameTurn") (var "Count") (count at:(last From)))` then read the uninitialised `(var "Count") = −1`, so the sow produced no actions and the relay `(moveAgain)` never fired → the turn wrongly passed at ply 1.
- **Fix:** stash the **pre-`m`** previous mover (`ctx.context.trial.lastMove()?.mover ?? 0`) into `frame.prevMover` in the then-fold ectx; `(is Prev …)` (compile.ts:2035) already prefers `ctx.frame.prevMover` when defined (mirrors `applyHypothetical`). So `"SameTurn"` now reads Java's `state.prev()` (the predecessor ply's mover), not the candidate move. Verified: Pereauni ply-0 sows (counts 0,3,3,2), mover stays P1, var Replay=6.
- **Result:** **Pereauni** 2 MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK, **Choro (Lango)** 2 MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK (both confirmed caused by this change via an env-gated A/B re-run: reverting prevMover restores their MOVE_MISMATCH). Lam Waladach / Fergen Gobale still MOVE_MISMATCH (deeper, game-specific sow capture bugs — not this).
- **The "regression" (Shogi RandomTrial_0, REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME→MOVE_MISMATCH @ ply 75) is a TIMING ARTIFACT, not real.** A/B re-run (`LUDII_NO_PREVMOVER=1` env-gate, since removed) shows Shogi mismatches at ply 75 **identically with AND without** the prevMover change → pre-existing. Shogi (549 moves, expensive `ifAfterwards:(IsInCheck)` per ply) is slow; under the 20 s `PER_TRIAL_MS` cap the 22:40 baseline sweep timed out BEFORE ply 75 (clean-so-far → bucketed REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME), while this sweep reached ply 75 under lighter load and surfaced the real, pre-existing mismatch. Promoting actually makes the baseline MORE accurate (Shogi's true outcome is the ply-75 mismatch). Shogi reads `"SameTurn"` only at its top-level play rule, never in a `then`, so the then-fold change cannot affect it. UNCOMMITTED.
- **Next target (surveyed read-only during the sweep; notes at /tmp/match-mode-target.md): real Match/Subgame mode.** 6 `(match …)` games (Grand Trictrac, Julbahar, Mahbouseh, Mughrabieh, Tavli, Bul) are ALL stuck at ply-0 MOVE_MISMATCH because TS stubs a match into a 3×3 placeholder (`lud-compiler.ts:230 matchPlaceholderGame`). Their standalone subgames already replay in TS (GrandTrictracSubgame OUTCOME_OK both trials), so only the match WRAPPER is missing. Full Java semantics captured (Match.java subcontext delegation + ActionNextInstance + Context.advanceInstance matchScore accumulation + shared RNG across rounds). ~12 trials, mostly bucket-neutral (only GrandTrictrac likely flips to OUTCOME_OK) but a genuinely-missing subsystem — large multi-file feature (compiler subgame-file resolution + a MatchGame + minimal subcontext + `(matchScore)`/`(count Trials)` ints + harness NextInstance matching). Schedule deliberately. Also surveyed: race/escape deep MOVE_MISMATCH is genuine STATE divergence (harness scripts Java's exact dice faces, so NOT RNG); WINNER_MISMATCH (205) is a spread long tail with empty details (needs per-game verbose runs). UNCOMMITTED.

## 2026-05-24 22:40 CEST — `Graph.makeFaces` now caps cells at `MAX_FACE_SIDES = 32` (Java parity), dropping giant spiral/perimeter channels that TS was wrongly counting as cells. BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE (22:15/22:27/22:39) → PROMOTED to rolling baseline `/tmp/cur-ofall-*` (22:40). vs prior baseline (21:45): MOVE_MISMATCH 1060→1058 (−2), OUTCOME_OK 1032→1034 (+2); WINNER_MISMATCH 205, REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 259, COMPILE_FAIL 2 unchanged. cmp IMPROVED 2 (both Kawade Kelia trials MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK) / REGRESSED 0 across all 2558 trials.** New baseline totals: OUTCOME_OK 1034 / WINNER_MISMATCH 205 / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 259 / MOVE_MISMATCH 1058 / COMPILE_FAIL 2.
- **Root cause of the race/escape ply-0 cluster (flagged as next target in the 21:45 entry): a board cell off-by-one.** Custom spiral/track boards built via `(merge {…})` produced one MORE cell in TS than Java (Kawade Kelia numSites **72 vs 71**, Panchi **61 vs 60**). The extra cell shifted hand-container site indexing by +1 (handSite P1 sat at 72 not 71), so the recorded track-entry move `from=71,to=1` referenced a site that no longer matched → no legal move generated → forced Pass at ply 0.
- **Diagnosis (face-sides histogram via a temporary `LUDII_DEBUG_MERGE` dump in operators.ts, since reverted):** Kawade Kelia faces = `{4: 71, 62: 1}` — 71 genuine quad cells PLUS one **62-sided** face (centroid at board centre = the big spiral channel); Panchi = `{4: 60, 46: 1}`. Java's `Graph.makeFaces` (Core/src/game/util/graph/Graph.java:1321) hard-caps face detection: `final int MAX_FACE_SIDES = 32` and the cycle-walk runs `while (vertIds.size() <= MAX_FACE_SIDES)`, so any cycle needing >32 vertices to close NEVER closes and is never recorded as a face. TS's half-edge cycle traversal (`graph.ts makeFaces`) had no such cap, so it kept the 62-/46-sided channels as cells. **NOT a vertex-dedup-metric issue** — an earlier hypothesis that TS over-merged a vertex pair (Euclidean vs Java's Manhattan `Vertex.coincident`) was disproven (the metric change had zero effect on cell counts).
- **Fix (`graph.ts makeFaces`):** skip cycles with `length > 32` in the face-keeping loop (added `const MAX_FACE_SIDES = 32; … if (cyc.length > MAX_FACE_SIDES) continue;`), applied AFTER outer-face (most-negative-area) detection so the perimeter-ring computation still sees every cycle. Verified: Kawade Kelia 72→**71**, Panchi 61→**60** (match Java); Tugi-Epfe (40, vertex-based board) and Pagade Kayi Ata Sixteen-handed (384, all-quad) unchanged; Kawade Kelia ply-0 now generates exactly the recorded `from=71→1` (was 4 wrong moves).
- **Also kept (faithfulness, validated in this same clean sweep): `graph.ts` vertex coincidence now uses Java's Manhattan L1 metric** (`|dx|+|dy| < tol`, helper `coincident()` citing `game.util.graph.Vertex.coincident`) in `addVertex`/`findVertex`, replacing the prior Euclidean `hypot ≤ tol`. Bucket-neutral on its own but strictly more faithful; the 0-regression sweep covers it.
- **Result:** Kawade Kelia fully resolved (both trials OUTCOME_OK). Panchi advanced **ply 0→15 and ply 3→22** (cleared the ply-0 barrier; now diverges deeper on a different bug — same bucket, real progress not captured by bucket-delta). The other 21:45-listed ply-0 race games (Grand Trictrac, Julbahar, Mughrabieh, Tavli, Mahbouseh, Pagade) are `(match …)` games or vertex-based and unaffected by this geometry fix. UNCOMMITTED.
- **Next target (surveyed read-only during the sweep; notes at /tmp/sow-moveagain-target.md): the sow/mancala repeat-turn (`moveAgain`) desync.** 211 sow MOVE_MISMATCH trials; NONE at ply 0 (first sow always matches), first divergence frequently a **mover (whose-turn) mismatch** = relay-sow continuation handled differently than Java (e.g. Pereauni ply 1: Java grants P1 a repeat turn after the ply-0 sow, TS ends the turn; Gabata Wuqro ply 2; Lam Waladach ply 4; Ti ply 2 opposite). CONFIRMED read-only that this is NOT a regression from the 21:45 move.ts from/to=origin change — `compileSow`'s internal `apply:` runs with `withFrame({from, to: capSite})` where capSite=landing hole, so `(count at:(to))` inside the relay guard correctly reads the last-sown hole, not the origin. Needs a runtime probe on Pereauni (engine free). UNCOMMITTED.

## 2026-05-24 21:45 CEST — `(forEach Site …)` inside a `(do … next:… (then …))` capture-then now FIRES, and the sow Move now reports `from`/`to` = its origin so `(last From)`/`(last To)` resolve. BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE (21:17/21:29/21:41) → PROMOTED to rolling baseline `/tmp/cur-ofall-*` (21:45). vs prior baseline (20:38): MOVE_MISMATCH 1067→1060 (−7), WINNER_MISMATCH 208→205 (−3), OUTCOME_OK 1024→1032 (+8), REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 257→259 (+2), COMPILE_FAIL 2 unchanged. cmp IMPROVED 10 (across both trials of several games) / REGRESSED 0.** New baseline totals: OUTCOME_OK 1032 / WINNER_MISMATCH 205 / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 259 / MOVE_MISMATCH 1060 / COMPILE_FAIL 2 (2558 trials). Distinct games improved: **Obridje** (MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK), **Raja Pasu Mandiri** (MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK), **Acedrex de los Cuatros Tiempos** (MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK), **Gabata (Oromo)** (MOVE_MISMATCH→REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME), **Asalto** + **Manalath** (WINNER_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK) — i.e. the fix helps the sow/mancala capture family broadly, plus chess/hunt/space games that use `forEach Site` in a do-then.
- **Two coupled bugs, both on the Pallankuli `(do (set Var "NumSowed" (count at:(last To))) next:(sow …) (then (and (forEach Site (sites Track from:(last From) to:(trackSite Move from:(last From) "Track" steps:(var "NumSowed"))) (if (= 4 (count at:(site))) (fromTo (from (site)) (to (handSite …)) count:4))) (set Var "NumSowed" 0))))` capture-then.** This is the "reach-4" range capture flagged as the open lead in the 20:38/19:51 entries.
  - **Bug 1 (`compile.ts` — `compileThen`/`compileEffectAction` forEach handlers):** `(forEach Site …)` was **silently dropped** inside a then. The forEach-kind dispatch handled `Player`/`Value` but `return`ed `undefined` for `Site` — deliberately gated, because a *move*-then `(forEach Site …)` (e.g. Damspel's `(move Pass (then (forEach Site …)))`) reads the PRE-move board and must NOT fold post-move. So Pallankuli's ply-6 reach-4 capture never ran: TS left hole 8 at 4 and the store empty.
  - **Fix 1 — scoped enable via a new `allowForEachSite` flag (4th param), threaded ONLY down the do-then path.** `compileDo` calls `compileThen(thenNode, env, false, /*allowForEachSite*/ true)` because its `applyThen` already advances state via `applyHypothetical` (so `(forEach Site …)` correctly reads the POST-sow board). The flag is propagated through `compileThen`→`compileEffectAction` (and its `and`/`or`/`if` recursions); when set, `Site` dispatches to `compileForEachEffect`. The Damspel *move*-then path passes `allowForEachSite=false` (default), so it stays gated — no regression there (confirmed: 0 regressed, and `board/war/leaping/diagonal/Damspel` unchanged).
  - **Bug 2 (`move.ts` — sow Move `from()`/`to()`):** with forEach Site now firing, the ply-19 capture OVER-captured. `(sites Track from:(last From) to:(trackSite Move from:(last From) …))` carves the just-sown stretch using `(last From)`; that reads `trial.lastMove().from()`. The sow Move has **no decision action** (its actions are all `ActionAddCount` with `from=-1`), so `from()` returned −1 → the `(sites Track from:-1 to:-1)` slice hit the whole-ring fallback → capture range was the entire board → over-capture.
  - **Fix 2 — sow Move reports `from`/`to` = its origin.** Added optional `fromSite`/`toSite` to `MoveInit` (+ fields, + propagation through `withConsequence`/`withPrependedActions` since `compileDo` does `.withPrependedActions(preActions)`); `compileMoves` `case "sow"` sets `fromSite=toSite=origin`. Restructured `from()`/`to()` to prefer a real `isDecision()` action, ELSE `fromSite`/`toSite`, ELSE the decisionIndex/`[0]` fallback action — the earlier `?? this.fromSite` attempt failed because the fallback `ActionAddCount.from()` returns −1 (a number, never `undefined`, so `??` never triggered). Now `(last From)` resolves to the selected hole; the slice `from:(last From) to:(trackSite …)` matches Java's exact site set.
  - **Result:** Pallankuli ply 6 and ply 19 deltas now match Java exactly (verified via /tmp/pallankuli-probe.mjs); Pallankuli advances ply 6 → 70 (was stuck at 6). Java `Sow.java` semantics confirmed read-only: `captureRule` defaults to `BooleanConstant(true)` and, with no `backtracking`/`forward` (Pallankuli's case), the `apply:` effect evaluates ONCE on a TempContext (`to`=last-sown hole); the multi-hole 4-count capture chain comes from the `(then (forEach Site …))` path — i.e. exactly this fix — so the previously "deferred ply-70 single-seed-capture" concern was a mis-read and is subsumed here. UNCOMMITTED.

Bechi's `CaptureMove` define is a *recursive* `(fromTo … (then (if (is Even (count at:("NextHole" …))) (and (fromTo …) (if … )))))` — a capture that, on landing, conditionally chains further captures down the row. Without then-execution only the FIRST hop ran, so multi-hole capture sequences silently stopped after one step; Bechi drifted from Java's board at ply 7.
- **Fix:** compile the `(then …)` child via `compileThen` and, when present, run it after the base from/to actions. Mirrors the established sow-then pattern (`compileSow`'s then-handling at ~line 10413): pre-advance a working `postState` by applying the base actions, synthesize a recorded `Move` whose decision action is an `ActionMove({from,to,count,transferCount})` (so `(last From)`/`(last To)` resolve correctly inside the chain), build a `then`-context with that move+state (`inThen=false` + manual pre-advance — NOT `inThen=true`, which would re-apply the synth move via the `(if)` handler's `postMoveContext` and double-count), then append the then-effect's actions and an `ActionSetNextPlayer` if it requested `(moveAgain)`. Blast radius: every `(fromTo … (then …))` site (capture-chain sows like Bechi/Kunama); 2 improvements / 0 regressions confirms nothing relied on the old then-dropping behaviour.
- **Investigated next (Daramuti) — NO track bug; the "track-direction" lead was a phantom.** Full read-only diagnosis this session: TS's `buildMancalaGraph` builds BOTH Daramuti tracks correctly — `TrackCCW="1,E,N,W"`→`[1,2,3,4,5,6,7,14,…,8]`, `TrackCW="7,W,N,E"`→`[7,6,5,4,3,2,1,8,…,14]`. Vertex numbering matches Java (leftStore=0, bottom 1–7, top 8–14, rightStore=15). The trial's first move sows `5→4→3→2→1` = TrackCW forward (recorded `[SetVar:Direction=2]` ⇒ the phase-0 `(or …)` chose the TrackCW branch); successor(5)=4 on TrackCW confirms TS's track is right. **The apparent "ply-0 drift" was an artifact of `/tmp/sow-drift.mjs`'s naive matcher** (`legal.find(from && to)`), which picks the FIRST of the two from/to-identical Select moves (TrackCCW) instead of the recorded TrackCW one. The real parity harness already disambiguates via `chooseMatch`→`recordedCountDelta` (replay-trials.mjs:554) and replays Daramuti correctly to ply 48/80. **Real Daramuti divergence is in the `"BetweenRounds"` phase** (deep, game-specific): trial-1 ply 48 Java `from=0→7` (loser redistributes from store) but TS offers only a P2 Pass (round-end mover/phase-transition divergence); trial-0 ply 80 Java `from=15→14` vs TS `from=15→13` (`RightMostEmpty`/`NextHole` destination off by one). Not a broad cluster — deferred. Fixed `/tmp/sow-drift.mjs` to disambiguate by recorded count-delta so it stops misreporting ply-0 drift on multi-track sow games. UNCOMMITTED.
- **Mancala MOVE_MISMATCH cluster survey (this session, read-only via fixed sow-drift.mjs).** 48 sow games use the `(forEach Site (sites Pn) (fromTo → (mapEntry Pn)))` store-sweep idiom; almost all are MOVE_MISMATCH (the whole Gabata family ×18, Rab' ×6, O An Quan, Kiuthi, Puhulmutu, Galatjang, Tuz, Duene, Pallankuli, Main Chongkak, Bay Khom, Gifia, Isolo, Mewegae, …). **But the cluster is HETEROGENEOUS — there is no single shared fix.** True first-drift plies: **O An Quan ply 0** (drift at s11, a site OUTSIDE the sow path → special "Quan"/mandarin end-hole start or scoring rule); **Kiuthi ply 6** (TS's legal from-set already wrong — cascaded from an earlier sow-apply drift); **Pallankuli ply 6** (a `(forEach Site (sites Track from:(last From) to:(trackSite Move from:(last From) "Track" steps:(var "NumSowed"))) (if (= 4 (count at:(site))) (fromTo → store)))` "reach-4" range capture did NOT fire — TS left hole 8 at 4 and store empty; root cause is likely the `(sites Track from: to:)` range or `(trackSite Move … steps:…)` index). Each needs game-specific capture-rule debugging. **Best single next bet: the `(sites Track from:X to:Y)` range + `(trackSite Move … steps:N)` machinery**, since the "capture along the just-sown stretch" pattern (Pallankuli-style) recurs; verify it produces Java's exact site set before chasing per-game capture predicates. **Ruled out (this session): `(do … next:… (then …))` is NOT a dropped-then bug** — `compileDo` (compile.ts:6718) executes its trailing then via `applyThen`→`withConsequence` (lines 6735-6798), so Pallankuli's capture-then IS attached; the failure is in the range/`trackSite`/`(last From)` evaluation inside it, not a missing then. (Pallankuli's capture is the then of `(do (set Var NumSowed (count at:(last To))) next:(sow …) (then (and (forEach Site (sites Track from:(last From) to:(trackSite Move from:(last From) "Track" steps:(var NumSowed))) (if (= 4 (count at:(site))) (fromTo → handSite)))(set Var NumSowed 0))))` — board matches Java through ply 5, capture mis-fires applying the ply-6 sow.) ## 2026-05-24 19:51 CEST — `(place … counts:{N})` plain placement now BROADCASTS `counts[0]` to every region site (was seeding only the first site of each region). Traced on Ngulungu (4×7 mancala, `(place "Seed1" (sites Bottom) counts:{3})` / `(place "Seed2" (sites Top) counts:{3})`): TS seeded only sites 0 and 21 with 3 instead of all 7 bottom (0-6) + 7 top (21-27). - **Fix:** `const c = perSite ? (stacked ? perSite[idx] : perSite[0]) : count;` — broadcast `counts[0]` to all region sites for a plain `(place)`, preserving per-site `counts[]` indexing ONLY for `(place Stack …)`. This mirrors Java exactly: `PlaceItem.java:156` sets `countFn = (counts == null) ? new IntConstant(1) : counts[0]` (a SINGLE function) and `evalFill` applies it to every region loc; per-site `counts[]` indexing is exclusive to `PlaceMonotonousStack`/`PlaceCustomStack` (the `(place Stack …)` path). Threaded a `stacked?: boolean` field through the `StartPlacement` interface + both region-push branches + the apply-loop destructure (parsed at line 2488: `head1.name === "Stack"`). Post-fix trace: Ngulungu seeds all 14 holes with 3, ply-0 sow fills holes 1,2,3 to count 4 (no spurious moveAgain), mover correctly passes to P2.
- **Blast radius:** exactly the 8 corpus games using `(place … counts:{})` — Ngulungu, Mwendo, Chiana wa Kunja, Misoro Tsoro (+Additional Capture), Musoro Tsoro, Quendo (Misoro/Musoro mismatch at deeper plies 13-58 for unrelated sow reasons, unchanged; Quendo stays WINNER_MISMATCH). All other start placements (201 sow games use `(set Count N to:(sites …))`, verified at line 3112 to already broadcast correctly; `(place Stack …)` per-site unchanged) are untouched — 0 corpus bucket changes confirms it.
- **Next target: sow-APPLY fidelity across the big `(set Count)` mancala cluster.** The dominant sow MOVE_MISMATCH residual is NOT seeding — it's seed distribution / capture (`apply:`) / track-direction / `moveAgain` drift. Two shapes observed (read-only baseline survey): (1) **mover divergence** (TS state mover ≠ recorded next mover after a sow — Tsoro/Deka/Tapata/Daramuti: a `moveAgain`-relay mismatch) and (2) **same-mover, different from-set** (Kiuthi ply 6 / O An Quan ply 8 / Bechi / Pallankuli: a hole's count fails the `(< 1 (count at:(from)))` gate because an EARLIER sow-apply silently drifted the board from Java's, even though from/to matched). ## 2026-05-24 19:08 CEST — `(forEach Piece)` no longer applies a sibling piece's generator to a piece DEFINED WITH NO MOVE RULE (Chessence's `(piece "King" Each)` — "kings do not move"). BUILD_EXIT=0, 500/500 tests. **3-shard sweep DONE (19:08) → PROMOTED to rolling baseline `/tmp/cur-ofall-*` (19:08). vs prior baseline (18:18): ALL buckets unchanged (OUTCOME_OK 1023 / WINNER_MISMATCH 207 / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 257 / MOVE_MISMATCH 1069 / COMPILE_FAIL 2, 2558 trials); cmp IMPROVED 0 / REGRESSED 0.** Bucket-neutral but strictly more faithful — promoted on faithfulness grounds (matches Java's empty-Component-generator semantics).
- **Bug (`compile.ts compileForEach` bare-dispatch arm + `ludeme-game.ts compilePieceMoves`):** the bare `(forEach Piece)` dispatch looked up each occupied site's component generator via `byWhat.get(what) ?? ownerGen`. A piece DEFINED WITH NO `<moves>` (Chessence's King) is registered in NEITHER `byOwner` NOR `byWhat`, so `byWhat.get(KingWhat)` was `undefined` and the lookup **fell back to `ownerGen` — the owner's sibling Pawn generator** — making the King emit the Pawn's Slide moves. Confirmed at Chessence trial-1 ply 25: site 48 = King2 (what 4), TS generated `48→49, 48→50` (a rook-slide from the king, valid because 48 is orthogonally adjacent to friendly Pawn2 at 42, passing the supported-pawn guard). Java calls each Component's own (empty) generator, so a no-move piece contributes nothing.
- **Fix:** added `componentsWithoutMoves: Set<number>` to `CompileEnv`; `compilePieceMoves` now records a piece's component `what` id(s) there when it has no `movesNode` (mirroring the role→what resolution used for the move-bearing case: `Each`→`${label}${p}` for each p, `Shared`/`Neutral`→bare label, `Pk`→`${label}${pid}`); the bare-dispatch arm skips any `what` in that set BEFORE the `ownerGen` fallback. The legitimate `ownerGen` fallback (unlabeled/single-piece games whose `what` simply isn't keyed) is untouched — only pieces *explicitly* defined move-less are excluded. Blast radius: games with a no-move piece type alongside a move-bearing sibling of the same owner under a bare `(forEach Piece)`; 0 corpus bucket changes confirms nothing relied on the buggy fallback.
- **Chessence residual is a STALE-TRIAL ARTIFACT, not a TS bug (corrects the 18:18 "supported-pawn mobility" hypothesis below).** After the fix, Chessence trial-1 ply 25 still generates 5 moves (hand-drops `55→49,43,44,38,37`) where Java records a forced Pass → stays MOVE_MISMATCH. Root cause: **both Chessence trials are `LUDII_VERSION=1.3.0`** while the current lud is `(version "1.3.14")`. Neither 1.3.0 trial ever exercises a hand-drop (`(grep) from=54/55 decision=true` = 0 hits across both) and both end in a forced pass — i.e. the 1.3.0 hand-drop branch produced no moves, whereas the current lud's `(if (is Occupied (handSite Mover)) (move (from (sites Hand)) (to (sites Mover "PawnStart") if:(is Empty (to)))))` legitimately drops onto the 5 empty PawnStart pits. Verified the divergence is NOT a check issue: King2 (site 48) is NOT threatened at ply 25 (0 enemy moves target it; branch A is correctly empty because P2's scattered pawns are unsupported), and Ludii `Game.moves()` applies no global check filter. So TS faithfully matches the *current* Java lud; the 1.3.0 trial is simply pre-rule-change. (Same stale-artifact class as the 18:18 Chessense→Chessence rename.)
- **Next target (read-only-surveyed during the sweep): the sow/mancala family.** A recurring turn-passing / relay-sowing `(moveAgain)` discrepancy at ply 0-2 across Ngulungu, Pereauni, Gabata (Wuqro), Ti, Choro (Lango), Chiana wa Kunja, Fergen Gobale, Mwendo (recorded mover ≠ TS mover immediately after the first sow) — TS and Java disagree on when a sow grants a continuation turn vs passes. No dice involved; large addressable cluster. UNCOMMITTED.

It is threaded through `loadGame(gameRelPath, trialPath)` → `resolveGamePath(gameRelPath, trialPath)` and only consulted after tiers 1+2 fail, **guarded by a `statSync` existence check** — so it can only resolve a genuinely-missing game to a real file and can never remap a trial that already resolves (tiers 1+2 are untouched). Blast radius: exactly the otherwise-COMPILE_FAIL renamed-game trials (here Chessence ×2); provably inert for every currently-resolving trial.

Chessence pawns may move only when orthogonally adjacent to a friendly pawn, gated by `(if (not (all Sites (sites Around (from) Orthogonal) if:(not ("IsPieceAt" "Pawn" Mover (site))))) (move Slide …))`. TS's `(all Sites … if:…)` correctly rebinds `(site)` per element (compile.ts:2766), so the over-generation is elsewhere — `(sites Around (from) Orthogonal)`, the `("IsPieceAt" "Pawn" Mover (site))` test, or the per-piece `(from)` binding — to be DEBUG_PLY-traced next.

- **Fix (two files, faithful to Java `game/functions/region/sites/crossing/SitesCrossing.java` + `Common main.math.MathRoutines.isCrossing`):** (a) `graph/trajectories.ts` — added `edgeEndpointPts(site)` returning the flat `[ax,ay,bx,by]` of an Edge play-site's two endpoint-vertex centroids (the geometry Java reads via `Edge.vA()/vB().centroid()`; complements the existing `edgeEndpoints` which returns the vertex *ids*). (b) `compile.ts compileSites` — new `case "Crossing"`: resolve `at:` to an edge index (default `LastTo`); resolve the `who`/`role` to `whoSiteId` (`All`/`Shared`/`Each` → `numPlayers+1`, else `resolveHandRole`; `whoSiteId==0` ⇒ empty, matching Java's non-graph-game `null`); then iterate every *other* edge `k`, keep it when the ownership filter passes — `whoSiteId==numPlayers+1` ⇒ `whatAtSite(k)!=0` (any piece), else `cellAt(k).owner==whoSiteId` — AND its segment geometrically crosses the at-edge. The crossing test is a line-for-line port of `MathRoutines.isCrossing` (parametric segment intersection with `det` parallel-guard at `EPSILON=1e-7` and the **exact 0.01 endpoint MARGIN**, so shared endpoints / T-junctions do NOT count as crossings — essential for Crossline's "crosses exactly one existing line" rule).
- **Verification / why zero real risk:** the change is purely **additive** — a new switch case that previously *threw*, plus a new accessor method. No existing code path is altered, so no game that doesn't use `(sites Crossing)` can change behaviour (the 3 chess/shogi flips above are independently provable no-ops). Direct filtered replay confirmed both Crossline trials COMPILE_FAIL→OUTCOME_OK *before* the sweep; the sweep confirmed it corpus-wide with 0 regressions. **Bonus:** CrossGeo and "Line and Cross" (experimental/graph_theory, not in the 2558-trial corpus baseline) now compile too — they advance from COMPILE_FAIL to deeper MOVE_MISMATCH (CrossGeo ply 4-7) / WINNER_MISMATCH (Line and Cross ply 22), separate per-game issues.
- **Deferred / remaining COMPILE_FAILs (4):** **Morpion Solitaire ×2** — needs dual-site-type state: it plays on **Vertex** sites but *draws lines* on **Edge** sub-elements (`(add (to Edge (edge (last From) (last To))))`, `(is Empty Edge …)`), and also the `(edge vA vB)` int ludeme (Java `functions/ints/iterator/Edge.findEdge`); the TS flat single-site-type state can't hold both vertex pieces and edge marks — a substantial feature. **Chessence ×2** — fails on a subtler unsupported construct (KnightWalk/Leap/hop/count-Steps/all-Sites all individually present; not yet pinned down). Real IMPROVED 4 (both Centrifugal Force trials + both Centripetal Force trials MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK), REGRESSED 0.** New baseline totals: OUTCOME_OK 1021 / WINNER_MISMATCH 207 / REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 257 / MOVE_MISMATCH 1067 / COMPILE_FAIL 6 (2558 trials). **Despite both fixes touching extremely common constructs (`(or …)` in countless games, `(count Pieces …)` in the default-role path), the sweep shows ZERO regressions** — strong evidence both are strictly-more-faithful corrections, not behavioural drift.

Java parity (`other/PlayersIndices.getIdPlayers`, `case All`): the player set for `All` is `for (pid = 0; pid <= players().size(); ++pid)` — **inclusive of player 0**, so Neutral pieces ARE counted. Net effect in Centrifugal/Centripetal Force, whose drop predicate is `(= 0 (count Pieces All in:(sites LineOfSight …)))` / `(= 1 (count Pieces in:(sites Around …)))` against the four neutral boundary squares: every count read 0, so the "line of sight is clear" / "exactly one neighbour" tests were mis-evaluated. **Fix:** the `everyone` branch now tests `ctx.state.isOccupiedSite(s)` (what-based occupancy, the same predicate behind `(sites Empty)`/`(is Occupied)`), so neutral pieces are counted; per-site count still contributes `Math.max(1, countAtSite(s))`. (`(count Pieces All)` on the 4-square board now returns 4, was 0.)
- **Bug 2 — `(or ( A B ))` collapsed to a conjunction (`compile.ts compileBool`).** Ludii's parser accepts the redundant-parens grouping idiom `(or ( A B ))` (Java `Or(a,b)` binary constructor — the inner headless paren-shell `( A B )` IS the operand list). TS's `case "or"` used `flattenMoveList`, which only flattens an explicit curly `{ … }`; the headless paren-shell fell through to `compileBool`'s default headless-paren handling, which is **implicit conjunction** — so `(or ( (or {Edge-drops}) (or {adjacency}) ))` evaluated as `Edge-drops AND adjacency` = their *intersection* (the 8 corner-adjacent cells) instead of their *union* (~44 edge cells incl. Java's recorded `to=159`). It only worked for `(and ( A B ))` by coincidence (the default already IS AND). **Fix:** new `flattenBoolList(items)` flattens a single curly list **or** a ≥2-item headless paren-shell into the operand list, used by both `case "and"` and `case "or"`. A genuine binary `(or A B)` arrives as length-2 items and is returned unchanged.
- **Diagnosis path (read-only probes `/tmp/dbg-cf-*.mjs`):** confirmed each of the 8 define-instances (`FromEdgeToEdge`×4, `DroppableFromDirection`×4) produces its correct site set individually, but the full `(or (…))` returned exactly the DFD intersection — isolating the two bugs. Centripetal Force was fixed by Bug 1 alone (single central neutral piece); Centrifugal Force needed both.
- **Deferred / next target:** the largest remaining MOVE_MISMATCH cluster is the **backgammon dice family** (~50+ `board/race/escape/*`: Portes, Plakoto, Fevga, Tavli, Backgammon, Senet, Grand Trictrac, Julbahar, Mughrabieh, …), which emit `from=N,to=N` no-op moves at ply 0 / wrong-mover or reversed-direction at deep plies — a shared track+dice subsystem issue (`forEach Die` / `NextSiteOnTrack` / `trackSite` are implemented but have subtle divergences), NOT touched by these fixes. UNCOMMITTED (stacked on the LineOfSight + place-random + graphend + edge-fix + sow-start + concentric + team-roles + Track from/to + moveAgain + place-on-Edge + forget + edge-play fixes).

(2) **Direction mis-parsed as type.** TS read the type via `positional.find(isIdent)` and the directions via `positional.find(isList)` — so a direction passed as a bare ident, e.g. Centrifugal Force's `(sites LineOfSight at:(site) S)` or Diagonals' `(sites LineOfSight at:(site) Diagonal)`, was swallowed as the "type" token and the direction silently dropped to all-Adjacent. Net effect on `(= 0 (count Pieces … in:(sites LineOfSight …)))`: TS counted pieces among *empty* sites in *adjacent* directions (vacuously 0/true), collapsing the drop/flank predicate and stranding the games at a ply-0 forced Pass.
- **Fix (`compile.ts`):** added `LOS_TYPE_IDENTS = {Piece,Empty,Farthest}`; the `LineOfSight` case now picks the type only from a `LOS_TYPE_IDENTS` ident (default `Piece`), and the direction node = the first positional that is neither a LoS-type nor a `SITE_TYPE_IDENTS` ident (honouring a bare ident, a `{…}` list, or a `(directions …)`), defaulting to `Adjacent`. Rewrote `lineOfSightSites(ctx, at, tokens, losType)` to implement all three Java modes faithfully — `Empty` (every empty site up to the first blocker), `Farthest` (the radial's last empty step, incl. the off-board end case), `Piece` (the first occupied site per direction). Affected corpus games (all already MM): Centrifugal/Centripetal Force, Diagonals (2002), Thales, Archimedes, Meridians, Tumbleweed, Squish, Millieu, Zombego, Hops-a-Daisy Generalized, Conflagration and Keep-a-riding, L'attaque, Labirintus.

With `from` already emptied by the outer sow, `available = countAtSite(from) = 0` ⇒ the nested sow produced **zero actions**, stranding the relayed seeds (TS hole 9 = 5 vs Java 2 in the traced Deka trial). The state drifts and the mover flips a ply or two later → MOVE_MISMATCH. The dominant *symptom* across the sow cluster (Java offers player N, TS only offers player M) has several distinct causes; this fix addresses the relay-capture subset.
- **Fix (`compile.ts compileSow`):** detect the leading non-string, non-SiteType-ident, non-`(then …)` positional as the `start` IntFunction (`compileInt`), and use `startFn.eval(ctx)` as the origin when present (else `ctx.frame.from`, unchanged for the ~all sows that omit it). The `(then …)` exclusion was **essential** — a first pass treated Andada's `(sow (then (if …)))` Then-clause as a start IntFunction and broke compilation for 8 games (Andada, Toguz Kumalak, Bao, Galatjang, Kapana Bona, Oure Ngat, Whyo, Bao Kiswahili → COMPILE_FAIL); excluding `(then …)` restored all of them.
