## 2026-05-23 00:49 CEST — checkmate/check detection lands for the chess + chaturanga + shogi family; `(move Promote …)` now resolves its named piece (was promoting pawns to the *King*). improved=29, regressed=3 (all 3 pre-existing-broken). OUTCOME_OK 835→851.
- **Two coordinated fix groups this checkpoint.** (1) `is Threatened` / king-safety filter now actually fires (carried from prior session: locate the named King component via `whatAtSite`, probe team-aware enemies, skip the `ifAfterwards` filter during `threatProbing` for perf, `(set Team)` start-rule for 4p/2-team variants). (2) **`(move Promote …)` and the promote piece-spec resolution — the fix that unblocked the cluster.**
- **Root cause of the regressions the threat fix *exposed*:** `(move Promote <site> <piece-spec> [player])` (compile.ts ~10324) **ignored its piece-spec entirely** and emitted `new ActionPromote(s, owner)`. `ActionPromote`'s `what` defaults to `who` (the owner id), and **component `id == owner` is that player's *first* piece — the King**. So every pawn promotion produced a phantom **second King**. With the king-safety filter previously dead this was latent (move sets still matched the recorded game); once `is Threatened` worked, the phantom king read as perpetually in check → the king-safety `(do (forEach Piece) ifAfterwards:(not IsInCheck))` over-filtered to ~1 move → MOVE_MISMATCH. Diagnosed on Rumi Shatranj via `DEBUG_PLY=99` (board showed `w1/p1` at **two** sites — site 4 = `King_noCross1` at start, so w1 = King — one of them in the promotion zone).
- **Java ground truth:** `Core/.../effect/Promote.java` `eval` resolves each named item to `components[].index()` (owner-matched when a player role is given) and emits **one Move per name** (`from==to==location`). `Slide.java` `eval` (line 184/221): `min = minFn.eval` is `UNDEFINED(-1)` when the `(between …)` has no `(range …)`, and the loop lands at `toIdx==1` — so a `(move Slide Diagonal …)` "jump-over-first" elephant **does** generate a distance-1 capture (confirms the Shatranj-Iraq king-in-check is faithful, not a false positive — see deferred).
- **Fixes (compile.ts, 3 edits + 1 helper):**
  - New module-level `extractPromotePieceSpec(spec)` → `{ names[], role? }`. Handles a bare string, `(piece "Queen")`, `(id "Queen" Role)`, the **nested** `(piece (id "Queen" Mover))` (Long Assize), and the **curly-set** `(piece {"Queen" "Knight" "Bishop" "Rook"})` (Unachess, where Java emits one move per choice).
  - `(move Promote …)` handler: resolve each name to its component `what` (player-specific `Name<pid>` then bare `Name`, owner from `componentOwnerById`), emit **one Move per resolved name**; owner-only fallback only when nothing resolves.
  - `(promote …)` consequent (head==="promote"): switched to the shared helper (`names[0]`); player role now also honours a role embedded in `(id "Name" Role)`.
- **Verification:** Rumi Shatranj → back to baseline REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME (phantom king gone); Long Assize → **2/2 OUTCOME_OK** (better than baseline); 1D Chess / Maharajah Chess → OUTCOME_OK. BUILD_EXIT=0, **500/500** unit tests.
- **Full sweep vs `/tmp/base-neutral-*` (22:24→re-run with `WALL_CLOCK_MS=1500000` for full coverage): 2558/2558 trials, 0 truncated. improved=29, regressed=3.** Buckets: OUTCOME_OK 835→**851** (+16), MOVE_MISMATCH 1327→**1311** (−16), WINNER_MISMATCH 208→**196** (−12), REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 182→**194** (+12), COMPILE_FAIL 6 (unchanged). **Every** improved and regressed trial is inside `board/war/replacement/checkmate` — no out-of-cluster effect, so the global Promote/threat changes broke nothing (draughts-king promotion etc. intact). Improvers span chaturanga (Chatur, Chaturanga, Kridakausalya 14x14, Shatranj×2, Shatranj Diwana Shah, Shodra, Hindustani, Tsatsarandi), chess (1D, Maharajah, Medieval, Long Assize, Double, Lombard, Level, Social Distance), shogi (Kyoto, Tai, Unashogi, Yonin Seireigi). New baseline `/tmp/base-neutral-*` re-snapshotted 00:49. UNCOMMITTED.
- **The 3 "regressions" are pre-existing-broken trials, not faithfulness losses:** all are WINNER_MISMATCH/REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME→MOVE_MISMATCH where the king-safety filter (now verified faithful per `Slide.java`) surfaces an **earlier silent board-drift** as a hard mismatch. **Shatranj (Iraq)** ×2: at the divergence ply the P1 elephant sits adjacent-diagonal to the P2 king and legitimately checks it (Java's slide also captures at distance 1), yet the recorded game made a non-escape move ⇒ the *replayed position itself* drifted from Java before that ply (this trial was already WINNER_MISMATCH/REPLAY-no-outcome at baseline). **Unachess** ×1: drop-chess; ply-15 divergence is a hand-placement issue, was already WINNER_MISMATCH. Both need a separate drift hunt, deferred.

## 2026-05-22 23:06 CEST — group-scoring games now compute scores: `(* (sizes Group P#))` folds the IntArrayFunction, and `(forEach Group …)` runs as a post-move then-consequent effect. improved=6, regressed=0
- **Two distinct group-scoring bugs, both fixed (compile.ts):**
  1. **`(* (sizes Group P#))` returned 0** — the *scalar* `compileInt` cases for `sizes`/`results` are stubs returning `0` (they're really IntArrayFunctions). A single non-curly array argument like `(sizes Group P1)` therefore compiled to scalar `0`, so Omega's end `(byScore {(score P1 (* (sizes Group P1))) (score P2 (* (sizes Group P2)))})` scored **0–0 → draw (winner 0)** where Java multiplies P#'s group sizes. **Java ground truth:** `game.functions.ints.math.{Add,Mul}` have an `@Or IntArrayFunction` constructor arm — `Mul` folds to the product (identity 1), `Add` to the sum (identity 0). **Fix:** in the `+`/`*`/`mul` cases, when the sole positional arg is a non-curly list whose head is an int-array producer (`INT_ARRAY_HEADS = {sizes, results, values}`), compile it via `compileRegion` and fold over its elements (`foldArrayArith`). Other ops (`-`/`/`/`%`) keep the scalar path (Java has no such arm for them).
  2. **`(forEach Group …)` as a then-consequent effect was a silent no-op** — `compileEffectAction`'s `forEach` case only routed `Value`; `Group` fell through to `undefined` and was dropped. Elea/etc. recompute scores each move via `(then (and (set Score Mover 0) (forEach Group Orthogonal if:(is Mover (who at:(to))) (if (is Odd (count Sites in:(sites))) (addScore Mover -1) (addScore Mover -2)))))`, so the whole `forEach Group` (and its `addScore`s) never ran → both scores 0 → draw. **Fix:** added a `Group` case to `compileForEachEffect` (mirrors the moves-path ForEachGroup: flood same-owner connected components via `aroundSites(dir)`, bind `(sites)`→group region and `(to)/(from)/(site)`→max site, apply the `if:` filter, run the sub-effect) and routed it in `compileEffectAction` with `inThen` → wrap the iteration in `postMoveContext` so the just-placed stone is grouped (Java runs consequents post-move). Body compile is **fail-soft** (unsupported inner effect → whole iteration no-op) so games like Bug/Lifeline (`(priority …)`/`(pass …)` bodies) keep compiling — matching the prior silent-skip.
- **Verification:** Omega RandomTrial_0 P2 product-of-group-sizes wins → winner 2 (rec 2 ✓); Elea final −3 vs −6 → winner 1 (rec 1 ✓), scores accumulate per ply.
- **Sweep vs `/tmp/base-neutral-*` (22:24): improved=6, regressed=0.** Omega ×1, Signum ×1, Elea ×2 WINNER_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK; board/space/blocking/Jostle ×2 MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK (also a `forEach Group` then-consequent). Net buckets (2558 trials): MOVE_MISMATCH 1329→**1327**, WINNER_MISMATCH 212→**208**, REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 182, OUTCOME_OK 829→**835**, COMPILE_FAIL **6 (unchanged** — a transient Bug/Lifeline COMPILE_FAIL from the strict effect-body compile was caught and made fail-soft before this sweep). BUILD_EXIT=0, **500/500** tests. New baseline `/tmp/base-neutral-*` re-snapshotted 23:06. UNCOMMITTED.
- **Deferred (deeper, same cluster):** Manifold/Brood (shape-matching `(results from:(sites Group …) to:(from) <expr>)` over from×to pairs + `(set Value …)`/`(value Piece …)` + `(priority …)` rotation matching), Spaiji/Manalath/Spao/Yoin (custom `(var "GroupN")`/connection win-detection). These don't use the simple `(sizes)`/per-group-`addScore` pattern — separate, larger features.

## 2026-05-22 22:24 CEST — then-consequent `(if)` branches now run their *effect* (not just the guard) on the post-move state; fixes the Diagonals over-count and eliminates the 22:14 "regression". improved=3, regressed=0
- **Follow-on to the 22:14 fix.** That change advanced only the `(if)` *guard* to the post-move state; the chosen *branch* (and its argument sub-functions) still ran on the pre-move state. So a then-effect like Diagonals' `(addScore Mover (count Sites in:(forEach diag if:(IsFriendAt (site)))))` under-counted by exactly the just-placed stone — a full diagonal whose only friendly stone was the one just dropped scored **0** instead of 1, and the corner/double-line bookkeeping drifted (observed 25–23 where Java ties).
- **Java ground truth:** the entire then-consequent — condition *and* result effect, with all nested sub-functions — is evaluated on the one post-action `Context`. There is no pre/post split within a consequent.
- **Fix (compile.ts, `compileEffectAction` `if`-branch, 2 edits):**
  - Run the chosen branch on the post-move ctx: `thenEff(guardCtx)` / `elseEff(guardCtx)` (was `…(ctx)`), where `guardCtx = inThen ? postMoveContext(ctx) : ctx`.
  - Compile the nested branches with `inThen=false` (was `inThen`) so a nested `(if …)` reads the already-advanced ctx **directly** instead of calling `postMoveContext` again — which would re-apply the recorded move and *double* it. Diagonals' `(if A (if B (addScore …)))` now applies the move exactly once across both levels.
  - Mirrored the same `inThen ? postMoveContext(ctx) : ctx` in the nested `(if <cond> (moveAgain))` sub-case so it too reads the post-move ctx exactly once.
- **Scope is deliberately narrow:** only effects **gated by an `(if)` inside a `then`** move to post-move evaluation. Bare then-effects (`sow`, custodial/surround captures, `(remove …)`, `(add …)`) still run on the pre-move `ectx` that `compileMoveLudeme` builds — so the documented `sow` pickup-state requirement is untouched.
- **Verification:** Diagonals RandomTrial_0 now 37–37 → tie (recorded tie ✓), RandomTrial_1 40–28 → P1 (recorded P1 ✓); Onek Rong still 2/2 OUTCOME_OK (its `(cost …)` arg is occupancy-independent, unaffected).

## 2026-05-22 22:14 CEST — `(move … (then …))` consequent guards now read the *post-move* state (Java semantics); + the graph-placement machinery (subdivide / Claim / per-site cost) lands. improved=26, regressed=1 (net +20 OUTCOME_OK)
- **Primary fix (compile.ts:8732, 1 edit):** `compileMoveLudeme` folded the `(then …)` consequent with `compileThen(thenNode, env)` — `inThen` defaulting to **false**, so an `(if <cond> <effect>)` guard inside a `then` was evaluated on the **pre-move** state. Java runs then-consequents *after* the move's own actions, so the guard must read the post-move position. Flipped to `compileThen(thenNode, env, true)`; the `inThen` machinery + `postMoveContext` (which re-applies the recorded move) already existed and is used by other call sites — this central one just wasn't passing the flag.
- **Why it was latent and not noticed:** with the pre-move guard, any `(then (if (… just-placed site …) <effect>))` mis-read the board where the just-claimed/-placed site is still empty. Games whose then-guard *doesn't* depend on the move's own effect were unaffected (same result pre/post), so the corpus mostly passed; only games that score/capture **because of** the placement diverged.
- **Java ground truth:** `Core/src/game/rules/play/moves/Moves` applies a move's actions, then evaluates its `then()` consequence on the resulting `Context`. `If.eval` (effect form) tests its condition on that post-action context.
- **Graph-placement machinery (carried from this session, first reflected in a sweep here):**
  - **`Graph.subdivide(min, cellMode)`** (graph/graph.ts) — faithful `@java Subdivide.java`: adds a centroid pivot per face with ≥`min` vertices, edges pivot→each face vertex; cell-mode rebuilds faces, vertex-mode clears the face/perimeter basis (matches `resetBasis`). Wired in board-graph.ts `case "subdivide"` (removed from the `PASSTHROUGH` no-op set). `(subdivide (hex 2))` → 24 hex vertices + 7 pivots = 31, append order preserved.
  - **`(move Claim …)`** (compile.ts) — faithful `@java Claim.java`: one `ActionAdd(to, what, owner, count:1)` per `(to <region> [if:…])` site, default piece = mover's; the `(then …)` is folded by the outer pipeline. Onek Rong previously generated only a `Pass` at ply 0 (MOVE_MISMATCH); now replays move-for-move.
  - **Per-site cost layer** — `State.costAt[]` + `costAtSite`/`withCostAt`, `ActionSetCost.apply` writes it, `(set Cost …)` start rule + `(cost [type] at:|in:)` reader (compile.ts), `(start (set Cost … at:|to:|in:))` (ludeme-game.ts). Costs seed independently of occupancy.
  - Together these take **graph_theory/Onek Rong** through MOVE_MISMATCH → WINNER_MISMATCH → **OUTCOME_OK** (the score `(addScore Mover (cost Vertex at:(last To)))` now lands because the `(= 1 (size Group at:(last To)))` guard reads the post-move board where the claimed vertex is occupied).
- **Sweep vs `/tmp/base-neutral-*` (21:45): same=2531, improved=26, regressed=1.** Net buckets (2558 trials): MOVE_MISMATCH 1349→**1330** (−19), WINNER_MISMATCH 220→**214** (−6), REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 176→181 (+5), OUTCOME_OK 807→**827** (+20). Improvers (mostly the post-move guard): Shiva, Two-Colour, 3 6 9, Diagonals(t1) WINNER→OUTCOME; Unnamed Dutch Draughts, Dama (Comoros/Philippines/—), Tama, Mastermind, Dala, Six Insect Game, Onek Rong MOVE→OUTCOME; Fang, Onyx MOVE/WINNER→REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME. BUILD_EXIT=0, **500/500** tests. New baseline `/tmp/base-neutral-*` re-snapshotted 22:14. UNCOMMITTED.
- **The one "regression" is a newly-EXPOSED pre-existing bug, not a faithfulness regression:** `board/race/score/Diagonals` RandomTrial_0 went OUTCOME_OK→WINNER_MISMATCH. Diagonals only scores when a diagonal becomes *full*, and the `(all Sites diag if:(is Occupied))` guard can only see the just-placed stone under the now-correct post-move semantics. Under the old pre-move guard the diagonal was **never** detected full → TS never scored → both players stayed 0 → a draw; RandomTrial_0's recorded result is *also* a draw, so its old pass was **coincidental**. Now TS computes scores (25–23) and diverges from the recorded tie. Its sibling RandomTrial_1 was correctly fixed (WINNER→OUTCOME).
- **Newly-exposed follow-up (deferred, deeper than this fix):** Diagonals over-counts by a hair (25–23 where Java ties). Root cause is a *second*, distinct state issue: the then-effect's **argument** `(count Sites in:(forEach diag if:(IsFriendAt (site))))` still evaluates on the pre-move state (the `inThen` fix advances only the `(if)` guard via `postMoveContext`, not the chosen branch's effect args), so a diagonal whose only friendly stone is the one just placed counts **0** instead of 1 (observed: two plies scored `+0`). Fixing this needs the *whole* then-consequent to run post-move while preserving the deliberate pre-move state that `sow` relies on — a broader change warranting its own sweep, tracked separately.

## 2026-05-22 21:45 CEST — `(sites Occupied by:Neutral)` / `by:Shared` now matches the TS neutral owner (`numPlayers+1`); fixed Nim/Chomp et al. that generated only a Pass at ply 0. improved=10, regressed=0
- **Root cause:** `ownerPredicate("Neutral"/"Shared")` (compile.ts ~4517) returned `(c) => c === 0`, written for Java's convention (`RoleType.Neutral(0)` / `Shared(Constants.NOBODY=0)`). But the TS engine registers neutral/shared **components** under owner `numPlayers+1` (`assignComponent`, ludeme-game.ts:1337) and seeds their sites' `who` to the same id via `seedOwner = numPlayers+1` (used pervasively by `ActionAddCount`/sow). So every seeded neutral/shared site has `who = numPlayers+1`, never 0 — and `(sites Occupied by:Neutral)` matched nothing, so the move generator produced no real moves and fell back to a lone Pass.
- **Symptom (ply-0 cluster):** 150 trials / 78 games where TS offers only `Pass` at ply 0 while Java has a real move. Nim is the cleanest: `(start (set Count … to:…))` seeds sites `o(numPlayers+1)/count>0`, the first move is `(move Select (from (sites Occupied by:Neutral)))`, which returned ∅ → Pass. Confirmed via `DEBUG_PLY=0`: board correctly seeded (`0:o3/h3 … 4:o3/h3` for the 5-pile default) but `by:Neutral` (expecting who==0) skipped all of them.
- **Java ground truth:** `Core/src/other/action/state/ActionSetCount.apply` writes `cs.setSite(state, to, who=UNDEFINED, what, count, …)` (who unchanged → stays 0) and `owned().add(piece.owner()=0, what, to)`; start `SetCount.eval` uses `what = components[length-1].index()`. So in Java the neutral occupant is owner 0. The TS port deliberately uses `numPlayers+1` as the single neutral/shared/seed owner everywhere else (component owner, `seedOwner`, sow `ActionAddCount`), so the faithful-to-the-TS-convention fix is to make the `by:` predicate match that id.
- **Fix (compile.ts:4517, 1 edit):** `case "Neutral"/"Shared": return (c, ctx) => c === 0 || c === ctx.context.game.numPlayers + 1;` — matches both Java's 0 and the TS neutral id. Paired with a what-based occupancy test, so empty sites (also who==0) stay excluded. No `whats`-seeding change needed: `whatAtSite` already falls back to `cells` (=seedOwner), and for single-neutral-piece games that fallback equals the component id.
- **Sweep vs `/tmp/base-tri-*`: same=2548, improved=10, regressed=0.** Nim ×2, Chomp ×2, Winkers ×2 MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK; Fibonacci Nim ×1, Traffic Lights ×1, Yavalanchor (WINNER→OUTCOME) ×1; Traffic Lights ×1 MOVE_MISMATCH→WINNER. BUILD_EXIT=0, **500/500** tests. New baseline `/tmp/base-neutral-*`. New aggregate buckets (2558 trials): MOVE_MISMATCH **1349**, WINNER_MISMATCH 220, OUTCOME_OK **807**, REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 176, COMPILE_FAIL 6. UNCOMMITTED.
- **Note on the broader ply-0 Pass-only cluster:** only ~10 of the 150 pass-only trials use `by:Neutral`/`by:Shared` specifically; the rest (graph_theory placement games, puzzle/planning, Pachisi-family hand entry, hunt geometry) generate no first move for *other* reasons — separate root causes, to be chased individually.

- **Change:** added `Graph.trim()` (faithful `@java Graph.trim()`: single high→low edge pass removing dangling-spur edges with live degree updates, then drop edgeless non-pivot vertices, renumbering) and wired `case "trim"` in `toGraph` to apply it (it was silently passed through unchanged via the `PASSTHROUGH` set).
- **Why it's safe + bucket-neutral:** `trim`'s blast radius is only the 10 corpus games using `(trim …)` (+1 wip, skipped). Verified all 10 are **bucket-identical** to `/tmp/base-tri-*` (no improve, no regress). Probed directly: `(trim (remove (rectangle 6 13) cells:{…}))` and `(trim (dual (square 5)))` return **identical** V/E/F to their un-trimmed operands — because TS `(remove … cells:{…})` keeps all vertices/edges (faithful: Java only trims edges under `trimEdges`) so there are **no orphan spurs to trim**, and `(dual …)` has none either. So trim never fires on the current corpus; those games (Archworm, Garrisons, There and Back, Stargazers, …) diverge for **other** reasons (the `RaggedSquare`/`RaggedTri`/`SymRemover` defines, option/adjacency), to be chased separately.

Locked with 2 unit tests (single-edge spur and a two-edge tail peeled in one pass). BUILD_EXIT=0, **500/500** tests (was 498; +2 trim tests). Baseline unchanged (`/tmp/base-tri-*`). UNCOMMITTED.

## 2026-05-22 21:05 CEST — `(tri …)` boards now use Java's compact graph numbering (was a sparse lattice): genTri made faithful to all four `*OnTri` generators + use:-dependent Cell/Vertex sizing. improved=11, regressed=0
- **Root cause (two compounding bugs):**
  1. **Wrong site-index numbering.** `(tri …)` boards routed through `parseTriBoard` → the **lattice** path (`triHexagonMask`/`triRectangleMask`/`triangleMask`), which numbers sites across the *full bounding box* with hole-punching (e.g. `(tri Hexagon 5)` → 81 box cells, 61 on-board, `traj=false`). Java builds tri boards as a **planar graph** numbered 0…N−1 by `graph.reorder()` (centroid order), so `(tri Hexagon 5)` is 61 vertices numbered 0–60. Recorded trials index sites by Java's compact numbering, so e.g. Hermit's ply-5 placement at site 26 hit an off-board hole in TS but a real vertex in Java → MOVE_MISMATCH. Affected 29 tri-board games.
  2. **`genTri` ignored `use:` and mis-shaped rectangle/diamond.** It hardcoded the **Cell** sizing (`a+1`) for every shape and `use:`, and built rectangle/square/diamond as a plain grid. Java's `*OnTri.eval` all add `+ (siteType == SiteType.Cell ? 1 : 0)` (so Vertex boards are one unit smaller), and use staggered/transposed geometry.
- **Java ground truth** (`Core/src/game/functions/graph/generators/basis/tri/`): `HexagonOnTri` d=dim+cell, rows=cols=2d−1, keep `col≤cols/2+row && row−col≤cols/2`; `TriangleOnTri` rows=cols=dim+cell, keep `r≤c`; `RectangleOnTri` rows=dimA+cell, cols=dimB+cell, c∈[0,cols+rows), keep `(r+1)/2≤c<cols+r/2`; `DiamondOnTri` uses a **different** xy `(hy·(c−r), (r+c)/2)` (hy=√3/2) — Diamond full rows×cols, Prism span (rows+cols−1)² keep `|r−c|<rows`. Default site type is **Cell** when `use:` absent (`Board.java:112 defaultSite = (use==null)?Cell:use`).
- **Fix (3 edits):**
  - `named-tilings.ts genTri`: added `vertexMode` param; `cell = vertexMode?0:1`; rewrote all four shape branches to the exact Java dims/keep/xy above (verified vertex counts: Hexagon 5→61, 3→19, 4→37; Triangle 5→15; Square 4→14; Rectangle 4×5→18; Diamond 5→25 — all match the Java keep-band formulas).
  - `board-graph.ts toGraph` `case "tri"`: pass `vertexMode` into `genTri`.
  - `ludeme-game.ts parseTriBoard(shape, board)`: route Hexagon/Triangle/Rectangle/Square/Diamond/Prism/unnamed through `buildBoardGraph` (compact `reorder()` numbering + real adjacency), `TRI_TILING`+`traj`, mirroring the existing `parseHexBoard` precedent; lattice mask kept as fallback for Star/Limping/custom-polygon and on build failure.
- **Default-site-type correction:** the old lattice always treated tri sites as the mask points (= vertices) regardless of `use:`; Java defaults to **Cell**. The two `(tri …)` topology unit tests encoded the old wrong vertex-default — updated to the faithful values (`(tri 4)` Cell→16 faces / Vertex→10; `(tri Hexagon 3)` Cell→54 / Vertex→19) and now pin **both** paths.
- **Sweep vs `/tmp/base-select-*`: same=2547, improved=11, regressed=0.** Y (Hex, a triangular board) MOVE_MISMATCH→OUTCOME_OK ×2, Jeson Zam ×2, Hermit (×1 OUTCOME_OK, ×1 WINNER), Inkblots/Faraday/Chains of Thought MOVE_MISMATCH→WINNER_MISMATCH. BUILD_EXIT=0, 498/498 tests. New baseline `/tmp/base-tri-*`. New aggregate buckets (2558 trials): COMPILE_FAIL 6, START_FAIL 0, MOVE_MISMATCH **1358**, WINNER_MISMATCH 220, OUTCOME_OK **798**, REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 176. UNCOMMITTED.
- **Newly-exposed follow-ups (deferred):** Hermit ply where it now reaches a WINNER_MISMATCH (end-rule, separate); Inkblots/Faraday/Chains of Thought now replay fully but end with the wrong winner (deeper end/scoring logic).

## 2026-05-22 20:14 CEST — End-rule `(if test A B)` was wrongly treated as a ternary (else-on-false): now matches Java `If.eval` (test-false → continue). All 13 "regressions" are illusory false-positive early-terminations
- **Root cause:** the end-rule `If` compiler treated `(if test A B)` as a ternary — `test ? A : B` — firing the 3rd arg `B` as an **else when test is false**. That ended games early (with a wrong/coincidental result) on every end rule of the form `(if cond (if sub (result …)) (result …))`.
- **Java ground truth (`Core/src/game/rules/end/If.java`):** signature `If(BooleanFunction test, @Opt @Or If sub, @Opt @Or If[] subs, @Opt Result result)`. `eval`: `if (test.eval(ctx)) { if (subs==null) return defaultResult; for(sub){ r=sub.eval(); if(r!=null) return r; } return defaultResult; } return null;`. So **test-FALSE → null → game CONTINUES** (no result); **test-TRUE → first non-null sub-If wins, else fall through to the 3rd-arg DEFAULT result**. The 3rd arg is a *default-when-test-passes*, **never** an else-when-test-fails.
- **Fix (compile.ts, two mirror edits):**
  - `compileIfResult` (~11234): `if (!cond.eval(ctx)) return undefined; const r = result(ctx); if (r) return r; return elseResult ? elseResult(ctx) : undefined;`
  - `compileEndResult` nested-if branch (~11331): identical shape (`!cond → undefined`, then `thenR`, then `elseR` as the pass-default).
- **Verified semantics on Odd (`board/space/group/Odd.lud`):** `(end (if (is Full) (if (is Odd (+ …P1 …P2)) (result P1 Win)) (result P2 Win)))` — now only resolves when the board is Full (was resolving early); trial 0 was already OUTCOME_OK in baseline (passing for the *right* reason now).
- **Sweep vs `/tmp/base-steps-*`: same=2545, improved=0, regressed=13.** **KEY finding — all 13 "regressions" are FALSE-POSITIVE early-termination removals, not real regressions.** Each regressed game's end rule had a now-removed spurious else-result that previously ended the game early and *coincidentally* matched the recorded winner. Verified by reading the end rules + `DEBUG_PLY`: Hermit `(if (all Passed) {…} (result Mover Win))`, Bug `(if (and (no Moves Mover) ("NewTurn")) (if (= (var "Last2Grow") (mover)) (result Mover Loss)) (result Mover Win))`, Netted `(if (or {…}) (if (is Triggered "Enmeshed" Next) (result Mover Win)) (result Mover Loss))` — recorded trials are long (48/17/12 moves) and the new divergences are at deeper plies (5/3/1). Since `EndRule.eval` cannot affect move generation, the exposed MOVE_MISMATCHes are **pre-existing** bugs the early-termination was masking. (Other 10: Abrobad, Claustro, OffShore, Polypods, Refugia, Wrigglers + the three above.)
- **Decision: KEEP** (faithful — reverting would reintroduce a known correctness bug solely to inflate the metric; per directive faithfulness outranks the bucket metric, and the 13 losses are illusory). BUILD_EXIT=0, 498/498 tests. New baseline `/tmp/base-endif-*`. New aggregate buckets (2558 trials): COMPILE_FAIL 8, START_FAIL 0, MOVE_MISMATCH 1367, WINNER_MISMATCH 214, OUTCOME_OK 793, REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME 176. UNCOMMITTED.
- **Newly-exposed follow-ups (deferred):** Hermit ply-5, Bug ply-3 (mover-desync), Netted ply-1, plus Abrobad/Claustro/OffShore/Polypods/Refugia/Wrigglers deeper move/turn/state bugs.

## 2026-05-22 — `(forEach Value …)` now fires in `(then …)` consequents (was silently dropped): faithful, 0 regressions
- **Root cause:** `compileEffectAction` (the then-consequent compiler) had cases for `sow`/`fromTo`/`remove`/`remember`/`forget`/`and`/`if`/… but **none for `forEach`**, so any `(forEach Value …)` arm inside a `(then (and {…}))` returned `undefined` and was silently skipped. In two-row mancala (Adi/Gabata/Qelat/…), the per-seed re-sow-restriction *release* is exactly such an arm: `(forEach Value (array (sites Track from:(last From) to:("NextHole" (last From) (min 12 (count at:(last From)))))) (if (is Mover P1) (if (is In (value) (values Remembered "P2SowFrom")) (forget Value "P2SowFrom" (value))) …))`. Because it never ran, the opponent's `PxSowFrom` set was never cleared, so holes the seeds passed through stayed (wrongly) un-sowable → wrong move set a few plies later.
- **Confirmed semantics:** Java `SitesTrack` with `from==to` returns just `[from]` (first loop breaks immediately on `site==to`), yet the recorded move *did* forget hole 12 — proving `(count at:(last From))` is the **pre-sow** count (5), i.e. all `(and {…})` then-arms eval against the same pre-application snapshot (generate-all-then-apply). TS already matches that (`effects.flatMap((fn) => fn(ctx))` passes the same `ctx` to every arm), so once the `forEach` arm is compiled it produces `to:(NextHole 5 5)=9` → `(sites Track from:5 to:9)=[5,6,12,11,10,9]` which includes 12. 
- **Fix:** added a `Value` branch to `compileForEachEffect` (explicit-list and `min:/max:` forms, binding `frame.value`), and a `head === "forEach"` case in `compileEffectAction` that routes **only** the `Value` iterator there (Site/Player/Die left as before — routing `Site` would re-introduce the documented promote-on-pass draughts divergence; see `compileThen` visit() comment).
- **Verified:** Adi ply-2 move now carries `ActionForgetValue`; replay advances **ply 3 → ply 85** (next divergence is a separate round-end/Pass issue). BUILD_EXIT=0, 498/498 tests.
- **Sweep vs `/tmp/base-steps-*`: same=2558, improved=0, regressed=0.** Bucket-neutral everywhere (no game's outcome bucket flips) but strictly more faithful — the only behavioural change is that `forEach Value` then-consequents now execute. **Decision: KEEP** (faithful, zero regressions; faithfulness outranks the bucket metric per directive). Baseline unchanged (`/tmp/base-steps-*`). UNCOMMITTED.
- **Newly-exposed follow-up (deferred):** Adi ply-85 — recorded move is `Pass` but TS still offers sow moves from the mover's row (round-end / no-legal-move semantics, likely the `"NoMoreCapturePossible"` collection + pass interplay).

- **FIX #1 — `(count MovesThisTurn)` (`movesThisTurn`, compile.ts):** was returning the *last recorded move's mover's* trailing streak, so at the start of a fresh turn it reported the previous player's count (1) instead of 0 → broke `(if (< 0 (count MovesThisTurn)) …)` continuation gates. Orthogonal`), and the `(count Steps)` parser extracts an optional RelationType token (default `Adjacent`, `Orthogonals`→`Orthogonal`). General — affects all `(count Steps)` on square boards.

## 2026-05-22 18:59 CEST — Per-piece facing (`Component.getDirn`) plumbed into relative-direction resolution: net +17 (improved 22 / regressed 5)
- **Root cause (Toads and Frogs `board/space/line`):** facing was resolved per-PLAYER only (`board.playerFacing`), but Ludii declares facing per-PIECE: `(piece "Toad" P1 E "MoveOfThePiece")`, `(piece "Frog" P2 W …)`. The Toad's `E` and the Frog's `W` facings were lost, so relative directions (`Forward`/`FL`/`FR`/…) rotated against a wrong/absent facing → wrong move set. Same class affects all draughts/shogi variants that set facing on the piece rather than (or in addition to) the player.
- **Java ground truth (`Component.getDirn()` + `Directions.convertToAbsolute`):** the relative→absolute rotation uses the **component's** direction (`dirn`), parsed from the 4th item of `(piece <name> <role> <dirn> …)`. Player facing is only a fallback.
- **Fix (faithful, plumbed end-to-end):**
  1. `ComponentTable.facingById: (string|undefined)[]` (ludeme-game.ts) — populated in `assignComponent` (new `facing?` param) from `collectPiece`, which now parses the facing token (4th item when a role is present) for the Each / Shared-Neutral / P-role placement paths.
  2. Game compiler binds `board.componentFacing = components.facingById`.
  3. `eval-context.ts`: `EvalContext.componentFacing?: readonly (string|undefined)[]`.
  4. `directions.ts` `facingForSite`: prefer the per-component facing (`componentFacing[whatAtSite(site)]`) when present, else fall back to player facing. Both lattice and graph relative-direction paths go through `facingForSite`, so the fix is uniform.
- **Sweep vs `/tmp/base-shared-*`: same=2531, improved=22, regressed=5; net +17.** Improvements: Toads and Frogs, Brazilian/Canadian/Italian/Philippines/American-Pool/Frisian Draughts, Dama & Dama-Kenya, Coc-Inbert, Dodgem, Maharajah Chess, Shatranj, Insanity (assorted MOVE_MISMATCH/WINNER→OUTCOME_OK).
- **KEY finding — all 5 "regressions" are FALSE-POSITIVE early-terminations, not real regressions.** HexDame×2, Seesaw Draughts×2, Dama (Philippines)×1. Proven via `DEBUG_PLY` + the harness's break-and-compare logic (`replay-trials.mjs:606` `if (game.over(ctx)) break;`, then winner compare at :770): WITHOUT facing the affected player was *paralyzed* (0 moves) → TS hit `(end ("NoMoves" Loss))` and ended the game early, coincidentally matching the recorded winner → spurious OUTCOME_OK. WITH correct facing the player now plays on, and the replay reaches deeper, PRE-EXISTING bugs (Seesaw distance-N diagonal capture not generated at ply 2; HexDame diverges at ply 9 on the rotated-hex board). The metric "regressed" but behaviour got strictly more faithful.
- **Decision: KEEP** (faithful + net positive; per the directive faithfulness outranks the bucket metric — and the 5 losses are illusory). BUILD_EXIT=0, 498/498 tests. New baseline `/tmp/base-facing-*`. UNCOMMITTED.
- **Newly-exposed real bugs (follow-up):** Seesaw distance-N diagonal capture generation (facing-independent; with P1@27, P2@34, site 41 empty the capture 27→41 over the enemy at 34 isn't generated); HexDame rotated-hex ply-9 divergence.

## 2026-05-22 18:32 CEST — Shared/Neutral-piece `(forEach Piece Shared)` move generation: net +2 (improved 2 / regressed 0), Slimetrail fully recovered
- **Root cause (Slimetrail `board/space/blocking`):** the lone Snail is a `Shared` piece (owner = `numPlayers+1` = 3 at runtime, per `collectPiece` ludeme-game.ts:1287). `(forEach Piece Shared)` found **0 moves** → engine emitted only a forced Pass. Two compounding gaps:
  1. **`compilePieceMoves` never registered the generator.** `playerOfRole("Shared")` returns `undefined` (only matches `P\d+`), so the Snail's compiled move generator was dropped from `env.pieceMovesByOwner`/`pieceMovesByWhat`.
  2. **The `(forEach Piece)` dispatch branch ignored the role.** It hard-scanned `cells[s] === ctx.mover` only, so even a registered Shared generator (owner 3) would never be reached on P1's turn.
- **Java ground truth (`ForEachPiece.java:278-298`):** `specificPlayer = player.eval(ctx)` (default Mover). For `specificPlayer == mover`, Shared (`> numPlayers`) or Neutral (`0`) → compute moves **directly without `setMover`**; only a real opponent player triggers `state.setMover`. So Shared/Neutral pieces are iterated but their moves belong to the **actual mover**.
- **Fix (two parts, faithful):**
  1. `compilePieceMoves` (ludeme-game.ts): role `Shared`/`Neutral` → register generator under owner `numPlayers+1` (matching `collectPiece`) and the **bare-label** component id.
  2. compile.ts: added `Shared`/`Neutral` to `FOREACH_PIECE_ROLE_NAMES`; `playersForRole` resolves them to `[numPlayers+1]`; new `foreachPieceSetsMover(owner,mover,numPlayers)` helper (`setMover` only for a real opponent `1..numPlayers`, never mover/Shared/Neutral); both the explicit-clause and dispatch branches of `compileForEach` now iterate `playersForRole(roleName,ctx)` and scan `cells[s] === owner`. Default (no role) → `[mover]`, identical to old behaviour.
- **Verified:** dbg-slime now generates the Snail's **6 Step moves** from the hex centre (was Pass-only).
- **Sweep vs `/tmp/base-sowthen-*`: same=2556, improved=2, regressed=0.** Slimetrail MOVE_MISMATCH→**OUTCOME_OK** (×2, full jump).
- **Decision: KEEP** (faithful + net +2, 0 regressions). BUILD_EXIT=0, 498/498 tests. New baseline `/tmp/base-shared-*`. UNCOMMITTED.
- **Same-symptom siblings BLOCKED on a different cause:** Quantum Leap, Shut Off His Lights also emit Pass-only at ply 0, but because of unimplemented **`(place Random {"Label"} count:N)`** — the `Random` ident isn't a string label so the start placement is silently dropped (empty board → no moves). RNG-parity cluster; deferred (needs Java `java.util.Random` reproduction to match recorded trials).

## 2026-05-22 18:50 CEST — Relay-sow nested `(then …)` fix: net +4 (improved 4 / regressed 0), Andada fully recovered
- **Root cause (Andada `board/sow/two_rows`):** the relay-sow consequent `(move Select … (then (sow (then (if (is Occupied ("NextHole" ("PlayFromLastHole"))) (moveAgain))))))` has a `(then …)` *inside the sow*. `compileEffectAction`'s sow case (compile.ts:8824) just called `compileSow` and **dropped the sow's own nested `(then …)`**, so the relay `(moveAgain)` never fired — TS handed the turn to P2 at ply 1 while Java kept P1 relaying. (dbg-andada confirmed: ply0 `ctx.mover=1`→ TS advanced to mover=2; recorded ply1 was `mover=1 from=7`.)
- **Java semantics nailed via `Move.toAfterSubsequents()` (Move.java:1081, returns the last action's `to`):** the inner then's `(last To afterConsequence:True)` resolves to the **enclosing Select move** (whose `to` is the *origin* hole), NOT the sow — because the sow's distribution is folded into the current move and isn't a committed trial entry yet when Java evaluates the sow's consequence. So at ply7 (sow from hole 1, landing hole 3): `NextHole(origin=1)=2` is occupied → `(moveAgain)` fires (relay continues); the **next** ply re-reads `(last To …)` off the now-committed sow move (landing 3) → pickup `NextHole(3)=4` is empty → P1 force-passes. Both the relay-continue and the terminal pass are reproduced exactly.
- **Fix (compile.ts sow case in `compileEffectAction`):** detect the sow's `(then …)` child, `compileThen` it; after distributing, swap in the **post-sow state only** (keep `ctx`'s recorded Select move so `(last To afterConsequence:True)` = origin), eval the inner then's effect + `moveAgain`/`moveAgainCond`, and append `ActionSetNextPlayer(mover)` when it holds (the action encoding of `(moveAgain)`, same pattern as the custodial-then case). Scoped to the **then-consequent** sow path; bare-sow generators (case 5531) and apply:-path sows unchanged.
  - First attempt recorded a *synthetic* sow move so `(last To)` returned the landing — WRONG (made `NextHole(landing=3)=4` empty → no moveAgain). Corrected per the Java timing above.
- **Verified:** Andada `RandomTrial_0` now replays **all 140 plies** with zero divergence (was diverging at ply 1, then ply 8 mid-fix).
- **Sweep vs `/tmp/base-graphouter-*`: same=2554, improved=4, regressed=0.** Andada MOVE_MISMATCH→**OUTCOME_OK** (×2, full jump); Whyo MOVE_MISMATCH→WINNER_MISMATCH (×2).
- **Decision: KEEP** (faithful + net +4, 0 regressions). BUILD_EXIT=0, 498/498 tests. New baseline `/tmp/base-sowthen-*`. UNCOMMITTED.
- **Note:** most remaining sow MOVE_MISMATCH (~248 in sow filter) are **four_rows** Bao-family (multi-track, different structure) — separate, harder cluster; two_rows relay games were the addressable subset here.

## 2026-05-22 17:29 CEST — Graph-aware `(sites Outer/Corners)` faithful fix: net +10 (improved 11 / regressed 1)
- **Root cause (from ConHex diagnosis):** `outerSites`/`cornerSites` (compile.ts) computed the perimeter ring & corners from the rectangular **bounding box** (`width/height` + `siteAt(x,y)`). Meaningless on graph boards (hex/triangle/merged/custom-poly): for ConHex `(sites Outer Vertex)` returned 6 garbage sites incl. interior site 12; `(sites Corners Vertex)` returned `[-1]`.
- **Java ground truth:** `MeasureGraph.measureInnerOuter` sets `OUTER == PERIMETER` for vertices (and marks a face OUTER when it touches a perimeter vertex). `SitesOuter`→`topology.outer(type)`, `SitesCorners`→`topology.corners(type)`. Corners come from `measureCorners`/`cornersFromPerimeter` (curvature of the boundary polygon).
- **Fix (faithful, lattice-safe):**
  1. `Graph.cornerVertices()` (graph.ts) — runs the EXISTING `cornersFromPerimeter` on the same CCW-oriented perimeter ring `measureSides` uses; returns vertex-corner ids.
  2. `Trajectories.cornerSites()` (trajectories.ts) — Vertex play → corner vertices; Cell play → `undefined` (cell corner edge/face logic not yet ported → caller keeps bbox).
  3. `EvalContext.cornerSites()` (eval-context.ts) — `traj?.cornerSites()`; `undefined` on lattices (no traj).
  4. compile.ts `outerSites` → prefer `ctx.board.perimeterSites()` (graph) else bbox; `cornerSites` → prefer `ctx.board.cornerSites()` else bbox.
  - **Lattices unaffected:** plain `(square N)` chess/tafl/sow boards have no `traj` → `perimeterSites`/`cornerSites` return `undefined` → existing bbox path. The 65 Outer / 74 Corners games on square boards are untouched.
- **Sweep vs guarded baseline: same=2546, improved=11, regressed=1.** Improvements (graph boards): Juroku Musashi×2, Shi Liu Kan Tsiang Kun×2, Ratio×2 (→OUTCOME_OK), Game of Dwarfs×2 (→WINNER), Chucka, Onyx (→WINNER), Unlace (→OUTCOME_OK).
- **ConHex did NOT recover (still MOVE_MISMATCH) — DEFERRED, deep:** ConHex's geometric graph (~288 verts, 92-vertex zigzag perimeter incl. non-play vertices) ≠ its 137-vertex play graph. Java measures topology on the reduced play graph (clean polygon); ours measures on the full geometric graph, so the zigzag perimeter defeats curvature corner-detection (smoothing pulls the +0.44 alternating scores to ~0.19 < 0.32 threshold → 0 corners). Recorded moves land on perimeter play-sites 9/36 that Java classifies as Corners. Needs the graph-reduction fix, not a corner-detection tweak.
- **1 regression — `hunt/Shui Yen Ho-Shang` (REPLAY_OK_NO_OUTCOME→MOVE_MISMATCH):** `(start (place "Marker1" (difference (sites Outer) …)))` on a MERGED board (rotate-45 square2 + square5); my perimeter places the right COUNT (15) but on slightly different vertices than Java → geese start shifted. Same merged-board perimeter-precision family as ConHex. Single trial; logged for follow-up.
- **Decision: KEEP** (faithful + net +10). BUILD_EXIT=0, 498/498 tests. New baseline: `/tmp/base-graphouter-*`. UNCOMMITTED.
- **Strategic note (corpus scan):** MOVE_MISMATCH is the dominant bucket (~1388). Biggest clusters: war/replacement 310 (chess/checkers — but diverge LATE, ply 159-294, rare endgame/draw edge cases, heterogeneous), sow ~250 (mancala — ~48 early ply≤2, relay/multi-lap continuation suspected), race/escape 139 (dice/backgammon). No single mega-fix obvious; each cluster needs per-game root-causing.

## 2026-05-22 16:48 CEST — `(to … if:)` per-destination filter fix (blocking cluster)
- **Root cause found:** the Add move generator in `compileMoveLudemeInner` (compile.ts ~9624) compiled `(to <region> if:<cond>)` but **silently dropped the `if:`** — so EVERY destination in the region was accepted. This over-generated placements and meant `(no Moves …)` end conditions never fired (ts=-1) for blocking games (Spots, Roots, …).
- **Java ground truth (Add.java:264-272):** `for toSite in region.eval: context.setTo(toSite); if (test == null || test.eval(context)) { add }` — `test = to.cond()` (the `If` named param of the `to` clause), evaluated per-destination with `(to)` bound to the candidate.
- **Fix (two paths, both compile `if:`→BoolFn and filter `region.eval(ctx)` by `cond.eval(ctx.withFrame({to:s}))`):**
  1. `compileMoveLudemeInner` Add `(to …)` branch (~9624) — the active path for `(move Add …)`.
  2. `resolveAddRegion` `to`-sub branch (~3066) — the bare `(add …)` effect path.
- **Also fixed `(sites Around … if:)`** (compile.ts ~3877): the around case ignored its `if:` named arg too. Now filters each neighbour with `cond.eval(ctx.withFrame({from:origin, to:neighbour}))`, faithful to SitesAround.java (`setFrom(site); setTo(to); cond.eval`). Needed because the blocking `if:` conditions nest `(sites Around (to) … if:(is In (to) …))`.
- **Verified:** Spots OUTCOME_OK (ts=2==2, 0 legal at final), Roots OUTCOME_OK (ts=1==1, 0 legal). BUILD_EXIT=0, **498/498 tests**. Blocking-filter run: OUTCOME_OK 51 (was lower); WINNER_MISMATCH 22.
- **Snowpaque (multi-phase) note:** still WINNER_MISMATCH (NOT a regression — was WINNER_MISMATCH ts=-1, now ts=1). Exposed a SEPARATE latent bug: phase "start" places `(piece (id "Marker0"))` which isn't counted by `(sites Occupied by:Neutral)`, so phase "play"'s `(= NumNeighbour 1)` filter sees 0 neutral neighbours and ends early. `(piece (id …))` owner-resolution gap — deferred.
- **NEXT:** full 3-shard sweep vs `/tmp/base-sitesstart-*` to confirm zero regressions + measure blocking improvement. UNCOMMITTED.

## 2026-05-22 16:07 CEST — Checkpoint: progress log created
- **State:** sites-Start component-keying fix complete, built, 498/498 tests, validated (5 improved / 0 regressed vs `/tmp/base-sitesstart-*`). UNCOMMITTED.

- **Root suspect:** `(is Connected N Sides)` in compile.ts hardcodes 4 compass sides (N/E/S/W). Java `Regions.convertStaticRegionOnLocs(Sides)` returns one region per entry in `topology.sides(type)` — a `Map<DirectionFacing, List<TopologyElement>>` whose size = actual board side count (triangle Y = 3 sides, hexagon = 6). So `(is Connected 3 Sides)` on a triangular Y board must connect the 3 real triangle sides; the TS 4-compass hardcode can never satisfy it.
- **Java side computation:** `MeasureGraph.measureSides` → perimeter runs between CORNER vertices → each run gets ONE compass side property via `discreteDirection(mid, avgRunPos, 16)`. So `topology.sides()` keys are a SUBSET of the 8 compass dirs, one per perimeter run between corners.

## 2026-05-22 16:18 CEST — Confirmed Sides bug empirically; implementing fix
- **Empirical (dbg-y.mjs on Y.lud):** Y board = 66 sites, sideRegions keys `['NW','S','NE']` (3 sides, 11 cells each — a triangle, NOT N/E/S/W). Replayed all 66/66 recorded moves; `game.over=false`, `winner=-1`, recorded winner=2. → win never detected because `(is Connected 3 Sides)` looks for N/E/S/W and 3 of those 4 fall back to wrong bbox cells.
- **Corpus scan of `(is Connected … Sides*)`:** `Sides` used ONLY by Y, Tabu Y, Master Y (triangles, 3 sides, corners count). `SidesNoCorners` used by Havannah, Havabu (hexagons, 6 sides, fork=connect any 3 non-corner sides). No square board uses bare `Sides` → fixing the Sides path can't regress squares.
- **Java faithful rule (Regions.convertStaticRegionOnLocs + IsConnected empty-filter):** goal regions = one per NON-EMPTY entry of `topology.sides()`. Triangle→3, square→4(N/E/S/W), hexagon→6. SidesNoCorners removes corner sites. Corner cells = cells appearing in ≥2 side regions (a corner vertex carries both adjacent sides in measureSides), used as faithful corner derivation without porting measureCorners.
- **Fix:** rewrite compile.ts `(is Connected)` so `Sides`/`SidesNoCorners` resolve goal regions at eval-time from all non-empty `ctx.board.sideRegions` (minus corners for NoCorners), falling back to the old 4-compass bbox only for lattice boards lacking sideRegions.
